  generated-client/            separate WSDL-generated client project
  target/                     Maven's generated build output
Modern Java needs the external SOAP libraries

Java 8 shipped the older JAX-WS tools and APIs. They were removed from the JDK in Java 11. A plain modern JDK does not supply jakarta.xml.ws.Endpoint or a wsimport command. This project obtains Metro and the Jakarta APIs through Maven. Do not compile the SOAP files with bare javac and expect those dependencies to appear.

mvn package

Wait for BUILD SUCCESS. The first build needs internet access unless all dependencies and plugins are already cached. It creates target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar. A successful build compiles the project; it does not prove that a server has started or that an HTTP request works. The examples are supplied as references: next, read and type the three beginner files before running them.

What matters in pom.xml?
The dependency already present in pom.xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>com.sun.xml.ws</groupId>
    <artifactId>jaxws-rt</artifactId>
    <version>4.0.3</version>
</dependency>

groupId identifies a library group, artifactId the library, and version the release. The compiler plugin targets Java 17. The shade plugin combines our classes and dependencies and preserves runtime service registrations. -cp will let Java locate this JAR; -jar runs its configured main class. You can inspect the complete supplied pom.xml without changing it.

3.4 Build and change your first SOAP service

Open src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/beginner in your editor. The three files below are also in the project. Type them into those files, save, and compare with the reference before building. Work in a personal copy of the course folder if several students share one computer. This small service has exactly one operation: send a name, receive a greeting.

src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/beginner/GreetingService.java
package com.aptech.session3.beginner;

import jakarta.jws.WebMethod;
import jakarta.jws.WebParam;
import jakarta.jws.WebResult;
import jakarta.jws.WebService;

@WebService(targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session3:beginner")
public interface GreetingService {
    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "message")
    String sayHello(@WebParam(name = "name") String name);
}
Line or labelWhat it means here
@WebService(targetNamespace = ...)Expose this interface as a service agreement. The namespace uniquely labels its XML vocabulary; it is not the web address.
public interface GreetingServiceDeclare the publicly visible agreement named GreetingService.
@WebMethodMark sayHello as a service operation.
@WebParam(name = "name")Give this input the XML element name name.
@WebResult(name = "message")Give the returned text the XML element name message.
String sayHello(...)Accept text and return text. The semicolon means the body belongs in an implementation.
src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/beginner/GreetingServiceImpl.java
package com.aptech.session3.beginner;

import jakarta.jws.WebService;

@WebService(endpointInterface = "com.aptech.session3.beginner.GreetingService")
public class GreetingServiceImpl implements GreetingService {
    @Override
    public String sayHello(String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}

endpointInterface points Metro to the agreement using its fully qualified name, including the package. implements GreetingService promises to fulfil it. The public sayHello method supplies the body. For the input Amina, "Hello, " + name + "!" produces Hello, Amina!. Metro puts that String into the SOAP response; the method does not build XML itself.

src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/beginner/GreetingPublisher.java
package com.aptech.session3.beginner;

import jakarta.xml.ws.Endpoint;

public class GreetingPublisher {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        int port = Integer.getInteger("soap.port", 8095);
        String address = "http://127.0.0.1:" + port + "/beginner";
        Endpoint endpoint = Endpoint.publish(address, new GreetingServiceImpl());
        Runtime.getRuntime().addShutdownHook(new Thread(endpoint::stop));
        System.out.println("Beginner SOAP service: " + address);
        System.out.println("WSDL: " + address + "?wsdl");
        System.out.println("Keep terminal A open. Press Ctrl+C to stop.");
    }
}
Publisher instructionPurpose
Integer.getInteger("soap.port", 8095)Read the Java system property soap.port; use 8095 if it is absent. This is a startup configuration shortcut, not reading keyboard input.
String address = ...Construct the endpoint URL: this computer, port 8095, path /beginner.
Endpoint.publish(address, new GreetingServiceImpl())Give Metro the address and the object that implements the service; start listening for requests.
Endpoint endpoint = ...Store the returned endpoint object so it can later be stopped.
addShutdownHook(new Thread(endpoint::stop))Register cleanup at JVM shutdown. Thread holds the cleanup task; endpoint::stop refers to its stop method. For now, keep this supplied lifecycle line.
System.out.printlnPrint startup information in terminal A. Printing a URL does not by itself start a service.

Terminal A — build and start: make sure you are in session3-soap-wsdl. After saving the Java files:

mvn package
java -cp target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar com.aptech.session3.beginner.GreetingPublisher

Expected startup lines include Beginner SOAP service: http://127.0.0.1:8095/beginner and WSDL: http://127.0.0.1:8095/beginner?wsdl. The prompt does not return because the server stays running. Leave A open. In a browser, open the beginner WSDL. Seeing XML confirms a reachable description, not yet a successful operation call.

Terminal B — send a request: open a second PowerShell window and enter the same project folder. This matters because curl reads the XML and headers from local files.

Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session3-soap-wsdl"
beginner-hello.xml
<soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/" xmlns:b="urn:aptech:session3:beginner">
    <soap:Body>
        <b:sayHello><name>Amina</name></b:sayHello>
    </soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>
curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8095/beginner -H "@soap-headers.txt" --data-binary "@beginner-hello.xml"

Expected: HTTP 200, with a SOAP Body containing sayHelloResponse and <message>Hello, Amina!</message>. Prefix names and whitespace may differ. curl.exe selects the real curl program on Windows; -i displays response headers, -X POST selects POST, -H "@soap-headers.txt" loads HTTP headers, and --data-binary "@beginner-hello.xml" sends that file’s contents. The @ is curl’s file-reading syntax, not a Java annotation.

WE CHANGE • PREDICT, THEN RUN
  1. In beginner-hello.xml, change Amina to Chidi. Predict the reply, save and send the same curl command. Expected: Hello, Chidi! No Java rebuild is needed for a request-file change.
  2. In beginner/GreetingServiceImpl.java change the returned text to "Welcome, " + name + ".". Predict the response. Save, press Ctrl+C in A, run mvn package, start the same beginner publisher, then send the request again.
  3. Record why the XML edit affected the next request immediately, while the Java edit needed a build and restart. Restore the original greeting and Amina before the later checks.
YOU TRY • LESS GUIDANCE

Add an operation String sayGoodbye(String name) to this beginner service. Decide which interface annotations to use, implement its body, and create a separate goodbye XML request using the contract’s namespace. Build, restart, inspect WSDL and send it. Acceptance: Amina receives Goodbye, Amina! inside a named result element. Do not rename sayHello. Write your plan before opening this hint.

Hint after you have attempted it

The agreement declares the method; the implementation supplies its public body. Use @WebMethod, @WebParam and @WebResult consistently. The SOAP Body’s operation wrapper must be sayGoodbye. A new method only in the implementation is not part of this explicit endpoint interface.

3.5 Read a SOAP message without guessing

XML is a text format with labelled elements. In <name>Amina</name>, name is the element name and Amina is its text. Opening and closing tags must match; elements must be properly nested. SOAP 1.1 is the version our service publishes by default. Its Envelope namespace is http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/.

say-hello.xml
<soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/" xmlns:s="urn:aptech:session3">
    <soap:Body><s:sayHello><name>Amina</name></s:sayHello></soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>
PartPurpose and rule
EnvelopeRequired outermost wrapper. Its namespace identifies the SOAP version.
HeaderOptional SOAP element before Body for extra message information. Our sample omits it. It is separate from HTTP headers.
BodyRequired element carrying the operation request or response.
s:sayHelloThe request operation in the service namespace urn:aptech:session3. This larger service uses a different namespace from the beginner service.
nameAn input child. It is unprefixed in this contract; follow the generated schema rather than adding s: to every element.
FaultAn error structure inside the response Body when a call fails. It replaces the normal result in these examples; it is not another required layer in every success response.

Namespaces and prefixes: xmlns:s="urn:aptech:session3" says that names starting with s: belong to that vocabulary. The prefix is a local abbreviation. Changing s to course everywhere with the same namespace does not change the operation’s identity. Changing the namespace does. The SOAP namespace is a URI identifier, not the address to POST to. A URI identifies something; a URL locates something, such as this endpoint.

An illustrative successful response — prefix/format may differ
<soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/">
    <soap:Body>
        <s:sayHelloResponse xmlns:s="urn:aptech:session3">
            <message>Hello, Amina! Welcome to Aptech Jakarta Web Services.</message>
        </s:sayHelloResponse>
    </soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>

HTTP carries the SOAP message. The request uses POST to /greeting. HTTP Content-Type: text/xml; charset=utf-8 describes its body’s format and encoding. SOAPAction: "" is the empty action used by this contract; soap-headers.txt preserves the literal quotes for PowerShell/curl. SOAP’s optional Header is inside XML and is not where those HTTP headers go. Other contracts may use a non-empty action. SOAP 1.2 uses a different Envelope namespace and usually application/soap+xml; do not mix versions in this lab.

YOU READ • THREE SMALL CHANGES

On paper: (1) rename the s prefix to campus while keeping its namespace; (2) change the name value to Ada; (3) change the operation to an invented unknown name. Which keeps the same call, which changes its argument, and which should fail? Then explain why a GET in the browser’s address bar can retrieve ?wsdl but does not send this operation’s POST body.

3.6 Read the WSDL as a service agreement

First stop the beginner server with Ctrl+C in A; it uses the same port as the larger service. Build and start the supplied larger course service:

mvn package
java -jar target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar

Expected: SOAP service: http://127.0.0.1:8095/greeting and a WSDL URL. In your browser open the course service WSDL. WSDL means Web Services Description Language. This is a WSDL 1.1 document describing how a client may call the service; it is not the result of a fee calculation.

WSDL itemQuestion it answersWhat to find here
definitionsWhich contract and namespace is this?targetNamespace urn:aptech:session3.
types / XML SchemaWhat shapes and value types do messages use?sayHello/name, calculateCourseFees/inputs, CourseInfo, CourseFault.
messageWhich parts form each input/output/fault message?Parts referencing the appropriate schema elements.
portTypeWhich operations exist and which messages do they use?GreetingService and its three operations. This is the abstract interface.
bindingHow are those abstract messages sent?SOAP over HTTP, document style, literal body use.
service → port → soap:addressWhere can a client reach this binding?AptechGreetingService, AptechGreetingPort, endpoint /greeting.

The schema describes valid data structures. XSD means XML Schema Definition. Metro may place it in a separate document and reference it with xsd:import schemaLocation="...". Follow that exact URL shown in your WSDL (often ending ?xsd=1) to find the data definitions. Different prefixes, ordering or formatting do not change the underlying agreement.

A small illustrative schema fragment to read
<xs:element name="calculateCourseFees" type="tns:calculateCourseFees"/>
<xs:complexType name="calculateCourseFees">
    <xs:sequence>
        <xs:element name="courseName" type="xs:string" minOccurs="0"/>
        <xs:element name="numberOfSemesters" type="xs:int"/>
    </xs:sequence>
</xs:complexType>

complexType describes a structured value with children. sequence gives their order: courseName before numberOfSemesters. xs:string is text; xs:int is a bounded whole number. minOccurs="0" permits absence in the schema. It does not mean your Java method accepts every absent/blank input: business validation may reject values that XML can represent.

The service uses document/literal wrapped: the SOAP Body carries a document described by the schema, with an operation wrapper containing its inputs. “Literal” means use schema-defined XML, rather than SOAP encoded object graphs. You do not have to hand-write WSDL here: this Java-first example lets Metro generate it from the annotated agreement. In a contract-first approach, developers begin with WSDL/XSD and generate compatible code.

YOU INVESTIGATE • CONTRACT WORKSHEET
  1. Find the service name, port name, target namespace and endpoint address. Copy their exact values into your log.
  2. Find all three operation names. For calculateCourseFees, record both inputs and the named result.
  3. Open the imported XSD. Identify the CourseInfo fields and their order.
  4. Explain what a client knows from WSDL and what remains a business rule in Java, such as the allowed semester range.

Do this in the actual document before viewing this check.

Check your findings

Service: AptechGreetingService; port: AptechGreetingPort; namespace: urn:aptech:session3. Operations: sayHello, calculateCourseFees, getCourseInfo. Fee inputs: courseName and numberOfSemesters; result: totalFees. CourseInfo order: code, feePerSemester, currency. The allowed range 1–8 comes from Java validation, not the xs:int type.

3.7 Understand values, objects and SOAP faults

A remote service exchanges messages, not references to live Java objects on another computer. Binding connects Java types to XML types. Metro uses Jakarta XML Binding, commonly called JAXB, for supported values. Marshalling converts Java data to XML; unmarshalling converts XML to Java data. We use the essentials here; Session 5 will study JAXB in depth.

Java type / ideaTypical XML mappingWhat a beginner should know
Stringxs:stringText, such as ADSE or a greeting.
intxs:intWhole numbers within a fixed range; our semester count and fee totals.
booleanxs:booleantrue/false values.
long, float, doublexs:long, xs:float, xs:doubleLarger integers or floating point values. Choose according to the contract.
java.math.BigDecimalxs:decimalUseful for decimal amounts; doubles may not represent decimal currency values exactly. Our illustrative fees use whole USD units.
byte[]xs:base64BinaryBinary content encoded for XML. Attachments/MTOM are further topics.
A JAXB-compatible bean such as CourseInfoA schema complexTypeNamed properties form structured data.
Arrays/lists of supported element typesRepeated schema elementsCheck the generated contract’s actual structure and optionality.
A declared application exceptionA WSDL fault and SOAP Fault detailClients can recognise a documented failure.

This is a useful map, not an exhaustive promise for every Java type. Database connections, Threads and open files are working resources, not suitable message data. Map-like or unusual types may require adapters or explicit data-transfer types. Optional/null behaviour depends on the generated schema; Java primitive int cannot be null. A DTO (data transfer object) groups values for messages, rather than carrying a database or server connection.

src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/GreetingService.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import jakarta.jws.WebMethod;
import jakarta.jws.WebParam;
import jakarta.jws.WebResult;
import jakarta.jws.WebService;
import jakarta.jws.soap.SOAPBinding;

@WebService(name = "GreetingService", targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session3")
@SOAPBinding(style = SOAPBinding.Style.DOCUMENT,
    use = SOAPBinding.Use.LITERAL,
    parameterStyle = SOAPBinding.ParameterStyle.WRAPPED)
public interface GreetingService {
    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "message")
    String sayHello(@WebParam(name = "name") String name) throws CourseFault;

    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "totalFees")
    int calculateCourseFees(@WebParam(name = "courseName") String courseName,
        @WebParam(name = "numberOfSemesters") int numberOfSemesters) throws CourseFault;

    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "course")
    CourseInfo getCourseInfo(@WebParam(name = "courseName") String courseName)
        throws CourseFault;
}

The larger interface explicitly fixes namespace, operation/result names and document/literal/wrapped style. throws CourseFault declares a possible checked application failure. Checked means a Java caller must handle it or declare that it may pass it onward. Returning CourseInfo makes the operation send structured data rather than one String.

src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/CourseInfo.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlType;

@XmlType(name = "CourseInfo", namespace = "urn:aptech:session3",
    propOrder = {"code", "feePerSemester", "currency"})
public class CourseInfo {
    private String code;
    private int feePerSemester;
    private String currency;

    public CourseInfo() { }

    public CourseInfo(String code, int feePerSemester, String currency) {
        this.code = code;
        this.feePerSemester = feePerSemester;
        this.currency = currency;
    }

    public String getCode() { return code; }
    public void setCode(String code) { this.code = code; }
    public int getFeePerSemester() { return feePerSemester; }
    public void setFeePerSemester(int feePerSemester) { this.feePerSemester = feePerSemester; }
    public String getCurrency() { return currency; }
    public void setCurrency(String currency) { this.currency = currency; }
}
CourseInfo codeMeaning
private fieldsStore each object’s code, feePerSemester and currency; callers use methods to access them.
No-argument constructor CourseInfo()Allows the binding layer to create an object and populate its properties.
Three-argument constructorConveniently creates populated objects in our implementation. this.code is the object’s field; code is the parameter.
getCode / setCode etc.Public getter/setter pairs let the binding layer read or fill these properties.
@XmlType(... propOrder = ...)Names the XML type and fixes the order of properties in this contract.
src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/GreetingServiceImpl.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import java.util.Locale;
import jakarta.jws.WebService;

@WebService(endpointInterface = "com.aptech.session3.GreetingService",
    targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session3",
    serviceName = "AptechGreetingService", portName = "AptechGreetingPort")
public class GreetingServiceImpl implements GreetingService {
    @Override
    public String sayHello(String name) throws CourseFault {
        String cleanName = name == null || name.isBlank() ? "Student" : name.trim();
        if (cleanName.length() > 60) {
            throw new CourseFault("Name must be at most 60 characters.");
        }
        return "Hello, " + cleanName + "! Welcome to Aptech Jakarta Web Services.";
    }

    @Override
    public int calculateCourseFees(String courseName, int numberOfSemesters)
            throws CourseFault {
        if (numberOfSemesters < 1 || numberOfSemesters > 8) {
            throw new CourseFault("Semesters must be between 1 and 8.");
        }
        CourseInfo course = getCourseInfo(courseName);
        return course.getFeePerSemester() * numberOfSemesters;
    }

    @Override
    public CourseInfo getCourseInfo(String courseName) throws CourseFault {
        String code = courseName == null ? "" : courseName.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
        if ("ADSE".equals(code)) {
            return new CourseInfo(code, 850, "USD");
        } else if ("DISM".equals(code)) {
            return new CourseInfo(code, 650, "USD");
        } else if ("CPISM".equals(code)) {
            return new CourseInfo(code, 500, "USD");
        }
        throw new CourseFault("Choose ADSE, DISM or CPISM.");
    }
}
Business rule / expressionPlain explanation
name == null || name.isBlank()If there is no name, or only blank text, use Student. || means “or” and stops once the first condition is true; this avoids calling isBlank on null.
condition ? first : secondA conditional expression: choose the first value when true, otherwise the second. Read it as a short if/else.
name.trim()Remove leading and trailing ordinary spaces before greeting. Reject names longer than 60 characters after this preparation.
semesters < 1 || semesters > 8Reject counts outside 1 through 8 inclusive before calculating. The actual parameter is numberOfSemesters.
getCourseInfo(courseName)Reuse one lookup method so the valid course names and rates stay in one place.
course.getFeePerSemester() * numberOfSemestersMultiply the returned rate by the count. ADSE has rate 850, so two semesters total 1700.
trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT)Remove surrounding ordinary spaces and normalise course codes without depending on the computer’s locale.
"ADSE".equals(code)Compare String content, not object identity. Choose ADSE, DISM or CPISM; reject all other codes.
Practice amounts, not published Aptech prices

These samples use ADSE 850 USD, DISM 650 USD and CPISM 500 USD per semester solely to practise arithmetic and contracts. They are illustrative values. There is no payment, database or saved enrollment in this project.

Read the Java error types, one step at a time
src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/CourseFault.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import jakarta.xml.ws.WebFault;

@WebFault(name = "CourseFault", targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session3")
public class CourseFault extends Exception {
    private final CourseFaultInfo faultInfo;

    public CourseFault(String message) {
        this(message, new CourseFaultInfo(message));
    }

    public CourseFault(String message, CourseFaultInfo faultInfo) {
        super(message);
        this.faultInfo = faultInfo;
    }

    public CourseFault(String message, CourseFaultInfo faultInfo, Throwable cause) {
        super(message, cause);
        this.faultInfo = faultInfo;
    }

    public CourseFaultInfo getFaultInfo() { return faultInfo; }
}
src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/CourseFaultInfo.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlType;

@XmlType(name = "CourseFaultInfo", namespace = "urn:aptech:session3")
public class CourseFaultInfo {
    private String reason;

    public CourseFaultInfo() { }
    public CourseFaultInfo(String reason) { this.reason = reason; }
    public String getReason() { return reason; }
    public void setReason(String reason) { this.reason = reason; }
}

CourseFault extends Exception makes an application-specific checked exception. super(message) passes text to the Exception constructor. final keeps the faultInfo reference from being reassigned after construction. @WebFault documents the fault’s XML name/namespace. The fault-info object carries a reason property with a no-argument constructor and getter/setter. The two-argument exception constructor lets Metro reconstruct CourseFault from a received message and detail object; the three-argument form also preserves an underlying cause. The one-argument form delegates using this(...) to create detail from the reason. throw new CourseFault(...) stops the normal method flow and reports the failure; it does not return a fee.

Faults are results to inspect, not something to hide. Zero semesters can be represented as xs:int, but violates our business rule. Metro translates CourseFault into a SOAP 1.1 Fault. Its Body has faultcode, faultstring and an application detail containing CourseFault/reason. In this implementation the HTTP response is 500. That HTTP status alone does not tell you whether the input was invalid or the server crashed: read the SOAP body. Malformed XML or an unknown operation may produce a runtime fault rather than our declared CourseFault.

YOU PREDICT • BEFORE SENDING

Write expected outcomes for ADSE/2, DISM/3, CPISM/1, ADSE/0, ADSE/9, and UNKNOWN/2. For each state success or fault, the total if any, and the rule responsible. Then predict the greeting for blank name text.

3.8 Send real SOAP and inspect real results

Keep the full /greeting server running in A. In B enter session3-soap-wsdl. You will test the real endpoint using two clients: curl and a Java HTTP program. Both send the same XML agreement. The browser simulator later on is a local demonstration; these commands actually call Metro.

soap-headers.txt
Content-Type: text/xml; charset=utf-8
SOAPAction: ""

This file contains HTTP headers, so it is not an XML or Java file. The empty SOAPAction value includes two literal quotation marks. Keep the file exactly as shown. A saved file helps avoid Windows-shell quoting mistakes.

curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8095/greeting -H "@soap-headers.txt" --data-binary "@say-hello.xml"

    <soap:Body>
        <s:calculateCourseFees>
            <courseName>ADSE</courseName>
            <numberOfSemesters>2</numberOfSemesters>
        </s:calculateCourseFees>
    </soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>

            <courseName>ADSE</courseName>
            <numberOfSemesters>0</numberOfSemesters>

</soap:Envelope>

src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/SoapRawRequestSender.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import java.net.URI;
import java.net.http.HttpClient;
import java.net.http.HttpRequest;
import java.net.http.HttpResponse;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.time.Duration;

public class SoapRawRequestSender {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        String filename = args.length == 0 ? "say-hello.xml" : args[0];
        String xml = Files.readString(Path.of(filename), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
        int port = Integer.getInteger("soap.port", 8095);
        URI address = URI.create("http://127.0.0.1:" + port + "/greeting");
        HttpClient client = HttpClient.newBuilder()
            .connectTimeout(Duration.ofSeconds(5)).build();
        HttpRequest request = HttpRequest.newBuilder(address)
            .timeout(Duration.ofSeconds(10))
            .header("Content-Type", "text/xml; charset=utf-8")
            .header("SOAPAction", "\"\"")
            .POST(HttpRequest.BodyPublishers.ofString(xml, StandardCharsets.UTF_8))
            .build();
        System.out.println("Request body from " + filename + ":\n" + xml);
        HttpResponse<String> response = client.send(request,
            HttpResponse.BodyHandlers.ofString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
        System.out.println("HTTP status: " + response.statusCode());
        System.out.println("Response body:\n" + response.body());
    }
}
Sender instructionIts purpose
args.length == 0 ? ... : args[0]Use say-hello.xml if no command-line argument is present; otherwise use the supplied filename. Index 0 means the first argument.
Files.readString(Path.of(filename), UTF_8)Read that local file as UTF-8 text, relative to B’s current folder.
HttpClient.newBuilder()...build()Create an HTTP client and a connection timeout. Chained builder methods configure an object before build creates it.
HttpRequest.newBuilder(address)Build a POST request, with headers, XML text body and a request timeout.
client.send(request, ...ofString(...))Send the request and read the response body as text. The program waits for this response.
statusCode() and body()Print both the status and the body, including HTTP 500 fault bodies.
java -cp target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar com.aptech.session3.SoapRawRequestSender calculate-fees.xml
java -cp target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar com.aptech.session3.SoapRawRequestSender invalid-fees.xml

Expected printed status: 200 for the first, 500 for the second, followed by their XML bodies. throws Exception on main is a lab shortcut: a file/network failure may terminate this command with an error trace. The sender prints SOAP faults as HTTP responses; it does not convert them into CourseFault Java exceptions.

YOU CHANGE • ONE INPUT AT A TIME

  2. Change only the count to 9. Predict the fault, then run again.

3.9 Call the service through a Java proxy

A proxy, often called a client stub, is a local object that represents a remote service. Calling its method looks similar to calling an ordinary object, but it sends a network message. The result still comes from the server. Your client is not given the server’s implementation object.

Client approachWhat you supplyWhen it helps
Raw HTTP/SOAP (previous lab)Address, headers and correctly shaped XML.See the messages directly; works without a SOAP code generator.
Typed proxy using a shared Java interface (this lab)WSDL names plus compatible Java interface and data types.A small controlled Java example where the interface is already available.
WSDL-generated typed client (next lab)WSDL plus generated Java types; no server implementation source.A partner client built from the published agreement.
Dispatch<Source> or Dispatch<SOAPMessage> (further study)A dynamic Jakarta XML Web Services client handling payload/messages.For flexible message-level calls. Not the same as our JDK HttpClient sender.
src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/SoapClientExample.java
package com.aptech.session3;

import java.net.URI;
import javax.xml.namespace.QName;
import jakarta.xml.ws.Service;

public class SoapClientExample {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        int port = Integer.getInteger("soap.port", 8095);
        String address = "http://127.0.0.1:" + port + "/greeting";
        QName serviceName = new QName("urn:aptech:session3", "AptechGreetingService");
        QName portName = new QName("urn:aptech:session3", "AptechGreetingPort");
        Service service = Service.create(URI.create(address + "?wsdl").toURL(), serviceName);
        GreetingService proxy = service.getPort(portName, GreetingService.class);
        System.out.println(proxy.sayHello("Amina"));
        System.out.println("ADSE, 2 semesters: " + proxy.calculateCourseFees("ADSE", 2) + " USD");
        CourseInfo course = proxy.getCourseInfo("CPISM");
        System.out.println(course.getCode() + ": " + course.getFeePerSemester()
            + " " + course.getCurrency() + " per semester");
        try {
            proxy.calculateCourseFees("ADSE", 0);
        } catch (CourseFault fault) {
            System.out.println("Rejected input: " + fault.getFaultInfo().getReason());
        }
    }
}
Client codeMeaning
QNameA qualified XML name: namespace URI plus local name. Use exact service and port names from WSDL.
javax.xml.namespace.QNameStill a JDK API with a javax name. Do not change it to jakarta; our SOAP APIs use jakarta.xml.ws.
Service.create(wsdlUrl, serviceName)Load the WSDL and create a client-side Service description for the named service.
getPort(portName, GreetingService.class)Create a typed proxy for this port. GreetingService.class identifies the Java interface type, not an implementation object.
proxy.calculateCourseFees("ADSE", 2)Metro marshals the inputs, sends SOAP, reads the response and returns the Java int.
try { ... } catch (CourseFault fault)Try the call; if the declared failure occurs, handle it and print its detail reason.
java -cp target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar com.aptech.session3.SoapClientExample
Expected terminal B output
Hello, Amina! Welcome to Aptech Jakarta Web Services.
ADSE, 2 semesters: 1700 USD
CPISM: 500 USD per semester
Rejected input: Semesters must be between 1 and 8.

The last call deliberately uses zero semesters and is handled by catch. Service metadata or transport failures can also occur; the lab main method lets other exceptions surface. In a real application you design timeout, retry and user-facing failure handling deliberately. A remote call can fail even when the Java method signature is correct.

YOU TRY • A DIFFERENT CLIENT CALL

In your copy of SoapClientExample, change the valid fee call to DISM/3 and the printed label to match. Predict 1950 USD. Save and rebuild; A may continue running because this edit only changes client code. Run the client from the newly built JAR. Explain why changing a client call did not require changing the service contract. Restore the sample afterwards.

3.10 Generate client stubs from the WSDL

Now build a client whose service types come from the contract. Stub generation means a tool reads WSDL/XSD and writes compatible client-side Java source. This is ordinary build tooling, not AI: it translates the published message agreement. You still write the client’s choices, user interaction and business workflow.

The supplied generated-client folder is a separate Maven project. It does not depend on the server’s Java source. The Metro jaxws-maven-plugin 4.0.3 runs the wsimport goal during generate-sources, which is part of mvn package. It fetches the WSDL and imported schemas from the running server, writes Java sources, and lets Maven compile/package them.

Key configuration already in generated-client/pom.xml
<plugin>
    <groupId>com.sun.xml.ws</groupId>
    <artifactId>jaxws-maven-plugin</artifactId>
    <version>4.0.3</version>
    <executions>
        <execution><goals><goal>wsimport</goal></goals></execution>
    </executions>
    <configuration>
        <wsdlUrls><wsdlUrl>${soap.wsdl}</wsdlUrl></wsdlUrls>
        <packageName>com.aptech.session3.generated</packageName>
        <keep>true</keep>
        <xnocompile>true</xnocompile>
    </configuration>
</plugin>

The Maven property soap.wsdl defaults to http://127.0.0.1:8095/greeting?wsdl. packageName sets the generated package. keep retains generated source; xnocompile leaves compilation to the normal Maven compiler. The complete supplied pom also includes Metro’s client runtime and the JAR-packaging configuration.

  1. Keep the full /greeting server running in A. Verify its WSDL opens before generating.
  2. In B, while inside session3-soap-wsdl, enter generated-client using the command below.
  3. Run mvn package and wait for BUILD SUCCESS. The first build may download the generator and dependencies.
  4. Inspect target/generated-sources/wsimport/com/aptech/session3/generated. Find the service class, service interface, CourseInfo and request/response types.
  5. Read the handwritten GeneratedGreetingClient below; run its newly built JAR.
Set-Location generated-client
mvn package
Get-ChildItem target/generated-sources/wsimport/com/aptech/session3/generated
generated-client/src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/client/GeneratedGreetingClient.java
package com.aptech.session3.client;

import java.net.URI;
import com.aptech.session3.generated.AptechGreetingService;
import com.aptech.session3.generated.GreetingService;
import com.aptech.session3.generated.CourseInfo;

public class GeneratedGreetingClient {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        int port = Integer.getInteger("soap.port", 8095);
        String address = "http://127.0.0.1:" + port + "/greeting";
        AptechGreetingService service = new AptechGreetingService(
            URI.create(address + "?wsdl").toURL());
        GreetingService proxy = service.getAptechGreetingPort();
        System.out.println(proxy.sayHello("Amina"));
        System.out.println("ADSE, 2 semesters: " + proxy.calculateCourseFees("ADSE", 2) + " USD");
        CourseInfo course = proxy.getCourseInfo("CPISM");
        System.out.println(course.getCode() + ": " + course.getFeePerSemester()
            + " " + course.getCurrency() + " per semester");
    }
}
java -jar target/session3-generated-client-1.0.jar
Expected generated-client output
Hello, Amina! Welcome to Aptech Jakarta Web Services.
ADSE, 2 semesters: 1700 USD
CPISM: 500 USD per semester

AptechGreetingService is generated from the service description; getAptechGreetingPort() returns the generated GreetingService proxy. Its CourseInfo type is generated in the client package. Sharing the same data shape does not make it the server’s exact Java class. The small handwritten client orchestrates calls; generated classes handle the contract plumbing.

Keep generated source disposable

Read files under target/generated-sources but do not edit them. Fix the agreed contract or your handwritten client, then regenerate. If the service operations or types change, rebuild/restart the server, verify its new WSDL, and run mvn clean package in generated-client to remove stale generated output. A greeting-text change alone does not change the signature/schema and normally needs no regeneration.

If the instructor changed the server port

Suppose A was started on 18095 instead of 8095. Supply the matching WSDL when building and the matching Java property when running:

mvn "-Dsoap.wsdl=http://127.0.0.1:18095/greeting?wsdl" clean package
java "-Dsoap.port=18095" -jar target/session3-generated-client-1.0.jar

The first property configures the build; the second configures the running client. Keep endpoints consistent. Do not change ports just to avoid understanding a connection error.

YOU INVESTIGATE • WHAT WAS GENERATED?

List five generated files and their purposes. Find the declared method for calculateCourseFees and its fault type. Explain how this separate client can call the service without copying GreetingServiceImpl. Return B to the server project with Set-Location .. before later raw-request commands.

3.11 Explain interoperability with evidence

Interoperability means systems built with different compatible languages or libraries can communicate using the same agreed message rules. A Java provider and a .NET or Python SOAP client need not share Java source. They do need to understand the WSDL/XSD, SOAP version, binding and address. Our raw curl client shows that the server can receive a standards-shaped message from outside a Java proxy.

Agreement to checkWhy it matters
SOAP version and HTTP media typeSOAP 1.1 and 1.2 have different namespace/media-type rules.
Namespace, operation and element namesWrong namespace or operation means a different/unrecognised message.
Schema types, order and optionalityThe client must represent inputs and read outputs in the agreed shape.
Binding and actionOur document/literal/wrapped binding and action must match.
Endpoint and network access127.0.0.1 points to the caller’s own computer. It cannot let a different computer reach your laptop’s loopback-only service.
Security and faultsA real partner must agree on authentication, certificates and supported extensions, and handle documented failures. This lab does not implement WS-Security.

Standards help compatibility; they do not guarantee every toolkit supports every extension. A document/literal contract using clear schema types is a practical starting point. This lesson verifies Java and raw HTTP callers on one computer. It does not claim that an untested .NET/Python integration works. Testing with the actual partner’s toolkit is how you prove that additional case.

YOU EXPLAIN • PARTNER SCENARIO

A partner has the WSDL and schema but cannot connect using http://127.0.0.1:8095/greeting from their computer. Explain why. Then name four contract facts to check before blaming the programming language. Separately, explain why switching the prefix s to p while keeping the same namespace is different from switching the SOAP 1.1 namespace to SOAP 1.2.

3.12 Follow the entire call, one step at a time

Before revealing the next step, say what you think happens. This trace is a browser teaching aid; it does not execute Java or contact the service. After you finish, connect each step to a real source file or request result from the labs.

    YOU DRAW • CLOSE THE TRACE

    Draw: caller → HTTP/SOAP → Metro → implementation → Java result or exception → SOAP response → caller. Place WSDL beside the contract preparation stage, not as the body of every fee call. Label which steps are your business code and which are runtime work. Explain what changes when the caller is a generated proxy.

    3.14 Diagnose a problem from its first useful clue

    Read the first meaningful error message and record the command, folder, expected result and actual result. Change one thing at a time, then rerun the same test. Keep these four stages separate: build → start → connect → interpret the response.

    SymptomLikely causeCheck and fix
    mvn is not recognisedMaven missing from PATH or terminal opened before setup.Use Session 2 setup; reopen B and confirm mvn -version.

    HTTP 404Wrong path, such as /greeting while beginner is running.Use /beginner or /greeting according to A. ?wsdl belongs on the description GET, not the operation POST.

    3.15 Assignment: build a library SOAP service yourself

    Build a small service that tells a library user their late-return charge. Start with a written agreement, then implement, publish and test it. Use this lesson as a reference for concepts, and write your own code without AI-generated solutions. Do not submit a renamed copy of the greeting service with no new behaviour.

    Where to build it

    In your personal copy of session3-soap-wsdl, create src/main/java/com/aptech/session3/library and use package com.aptech.session3.library. Keep using the supplied Maven dependencies. Create your own LibraryService interface, LibraryServiceImpl, LibraryPublisher, LibraryFault and LibraryFaultInfo. Do not overwrite the reference service. Publish at http://127.0.0.1:8097/library with namespace urn:aptech:library. Give the WSDL service/port explicit names of your choosing and document them.

    OperationRequired behaviour
    String getLibraryHours()Return Mon–Fri 09:00–17:00. Choose result element name hours.
    String welcomeMember(String memberName)Trim the name. Reject null, blank or more than 40 characters with LibraryFault. Return Welcome, {name}, to the library. Choose result name message.
    int calculateLateCharge(int daysLate)Allow 0–30 inclusive; charge 100 illustrative NGN per day. Outside that range, throw LibraryFault with a useful reason. Choose result name chargeNGN.

    These are practice rules, not a real library policy. Declare appropriate input/result names, preserve a document/literal contract and expose documented fault details. Your no-input operation still needs its correctly namespaced operation wrapper in the SOAP Body.

    TestExpected result
    getLibraryHours200; hours Mon–Fri 09:00–17:00.
    welcomeMember with Amina200; Welcome, Amina, to the library.
    welcomeMember with surrounding spaces200; trimmed name in message.
    welcomeMember with spaces only; 41-character nameA declared SOAP fault for each.
    calculateLateCharge 0; 3; 30200; 0, 300, 3000 NGN respectively.
    calculateLateCharge -1; 31A declared SOAP fault for each.
    An unknown operation or broken XMLA runtime failure to diagnose; distinguish it from LibraryFault.

    Test both boundaries, not just one convenient success. Record actual HTTP status/body, name the rule, and explain any difference from your prediction. Build a Java typed client as well as raw XML requests. For the typed client you may use a compatible shared interface approach or a separate WSDL-generated Maven project; document which you chose. If you choose generation, keep your handwritten client separate from generated source and set its WSDL service/port names from your own contract.

    mvn package
    java -cp target/session3-soap-wsdl-1.0.jar com.aptech.session3.library.LibraryPublisher

    3.17 Terms you can return to

    Use this as a reminder after seeing each word in a working example. If a definition is unclear, go back to the related section and explain a concrete call.

    JAX-WS / Jakarta XML Web Services

    Java APIs and mapping rules for XML web services, commonly SOAP. Metro implements these rules here.

    SOAP

    A protocol specifying XML message rules. Envelope identifies the SOAP version; Body carries a task, result or fault.

    Runtime / dependency

    Runtime software performs service work while running. A dependency is a library needed by the project; Maven downloads Metro and its libraries.

    Interface / implementation / object

    An interface declares callable methods. An implementing class supplies bodies. An object is an instance of a class.

    Annotation / metadata

    An @ label carries information about code. @WebParam names XML input; @Override asks the compiler to check a matching method.

    Endpoint / port

    Endpoint is the service’s reachable address/object. TCP port 8095 is a network number; a WSDL port such as AptechGreetingPort is a named endpoint definition.

    XML element / attribute

    An element has tags and contents. An attribute adds information inside an opening tag, such as xmlns:s.

    Namespace / prefix / QName

    Namespace identifies a vocabulary; prefix is its local shorthand. QName combines namespace URI and local name, such as the WSDL service name.

    HTTP header / SOAP Header

    HTTP headers precede the XML body on the network. SOAP Header is an optional element inside the Envelope. They are distinct layers.

    WSDL / contract

    Web Services Description Language documents the service agreement: types, messages, operations, binding and address.

    XSD / schema / complexType

    XML Schema Definition describes data rules. A complexType can group child fields, such as CourseInfo.

    Document / literal / wrapped

    The Body carries a schema-defined document with an operation wrapper around input fields in this project.

    Java-first / contract-first

    Java-first generates a description from annotated Java. Contract-first starts with an agreed WSDL/schema and generates compatible code.

    DTO / bean / getter / setter

    A DTO carries values between programs. Our bean exposes named properties with getters to read and setters to fill them.

    JAXB / marshal / unmarshal

    Jakarta XML Binding maps supported Java data and XML. Marshalling writes XML from Java; unmarshalling reads Java values from XML.

    Checked exception / throw / throws / catch

    A checked exception must be handled or declared by Java callers. throw raises a failure; throws declares it; catch handles it.

    SOAP Fault / fault detail

    A structured error in the response Body. Our application detail carries CourseFaultInfo.reason, separate from a normal result.

    Proxy / stub / wsimport

    A proxy is a local stand-in for a remote service. wsimport reads WSDL/XSD and generates client Java types used by a typed proxy.

    Interoperability

    Compatible systems communicate through the same agreed wire contract, even with different languages/toolkits. Verify the actual integration.

    Build / classpath / system property

    A build compiles/packages source. Classpath tells Java where classes are. -Dsoap.port=18095 supplies a startup property to our program.

    4.1 Plan a library service before typing

    A receptionist adds a fictional book. A browser reads it as JSON. A partner SOAP client asks for its title. Both interfaces use the same stored record. A separate print-charge calculation uses practice rules: 10 illustrative NGN per page, with 1–20 pages allowed.

    Request / operationResponsible codeExpected outcome
    POST /api/books with a JSON titleBookResource → BookStore.add → transaction201 and Location after commit; invalid title → 400.
    GET /api/books/{id}BookResource → BookStore.find200 id/title, 404 absent positive id, 400 zero/negative id.
    GET /api/books?title=...BookStore JPQL with a bound value200 array of exact-title matches.
    SOAP getBookTitle(bookId)LibraryServiceImpl → the same BookStore.findSOAP title for a saved row, declared LibraryFault for invalid/absent id.
    SOAP calculatePrintCharge(pages)LibraryServiceImpl validation and arithmetic3 → 30; 20 → 200; 0/21 → fault. No book row is written.

    You draw: REST client → Jersey → resource → store → Hibernate → H2. Then draw SOAP client → Metro → implementation → the same store. REST and SOAP do not call one another here. They share application data through the repository and database.

    Write predictions for an empty collection, one successful insert, a blank title, a missing id and a zero-page request. Keep the distinction between a Java method, HTTP method, database column and SOAP operation.

    4.2 Recreate the rule in plain Java first

    This warm-up uses only the JDK. Open beginner/PrintChargePractice.java. Before running, predict each of the five results. The method returns a number for valid pages and raises an exception otherwise.

    session4-practice/beginner/PrintChargePractice.java
    public class PrintChargePractice {
    public static int charge(int pages) {
        if (pages < 1 || pages > 20) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Pages must be between 1 and 20.");
        }
        return pages * 10;
    }

    public static void main(String[] args) {
        int[] pageCounts = {1, 3, 20, 0, 21};
        for (int pages : pageCounts) {
            try {
                System.out.println(pages + " pages: " + charge(pages) + " NGN");
            } catch (IllegalArgumentException problem) {
                System.out.println(pages + " pages: " + problem.getMessage());
            }
        }
    }
}
    CodeRead it in ordinary words
    pages < 1 || pages > 20Outside the allowed range. Check both ends before multiplying.
    return pages * 10Send the calculated number to the caller; it does not print by itself.
    int[] pageCounts / for (int pages : pageCounts)An array of inputs; the enhanced for loop takes each input in turn.
    try / catchTry the calculation; when it rejects input, print the reason and continue with the next array entry.
    Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session4-practice"
    javac beginner/PrintChargePractice.java
    java -cp beginner PrintChargePractice
    Expected warm-up output
    1 pages: 10 NGN
3 pages: 30 NGN
20 pages: 200 NGN
0 pages: Pages must be between 1 and 20.
21 pages: Pages must be between 1 and 20.

    -cp beginner tells Java where the compiled class is. These commands assume the project folder, not the beginner subfolder. The illustrative charges are not a real library policy or payment system.

    WE DO → YOU CHANGE

    In your warm-up copy change the rate to 12. Predict 1, 3 and 20 pages, then save, compile and run again. Restore it to 10 before comparing with the reference service.

    YOU DO • RECREATE A SIMILAR RULE

    From a blank BadgeCostPractice.java, write a returning method for 1–15 badges at 7 illustrative NGN each. Test 1, 15, 0 and 16 and explain each branch. Use your notes; keep the completed print-charge source closed.

    4.3 Keep files, terminals and addresses straight

    PartLocation / valueWhy it matters

    Use JDK 17+ and Maven from 2.3. Stop older practice servers before rebuilding their JAR. In your project copy, type or retype the relevant source file in its editor tab; replace its existing content rather than adding a second class/method with the same name.

    mvn package

    Wait for BUILD SUCCESS. The first build needs dependencies downloaded; offline builds need a complete cache. The supplied pom combines Jersey 3.1.5, Metro 4.0.3, Jakarta Persistence 3.1.0, Hibernate 6.6.5.Final and H2 2.3.232. It uses Metro’s JAXB runtime for both XML binding and Hibernate, avoiding two overlapping runtime implementations.

    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/LibraryDatabase.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.util.Map;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityManagerFactory;
import jakarta.persistence.Persistence;

public class LibraryDatabase {
    public static EntityManagerFactory open() throws Exception {
        Path base = Path.of(System.getProperty("practice.db", "./data/library"))
            .toAbsolutePath().normalize();
        Files.createDirectories(base.getParent());
        System.out.println("Library database: " + base + ".mv.db");
        return Persistence.createEntityManagerFactory("library-practice",
            Map.of("jakarta.persistence.jdbc.url", "jdbc:h2:file:" + base.toString().replace('\\', '/')));
    }
}

    Persistence XML version 3.0 describes the XML format used here; it is compatible with the 3.1 API. It is not a request to replace the selected API dependency. LibraryDatabase resolves the file location, creates its parent folder and supplies the JDBC URL. The H2 sa/empty-password account, built-in Hibernate pool and automatic schema update are local teaching settings.

    Terminal A runs the provider and stays occupied. Terminal B sends requests, edits request files and builds/runs the separate client. Console database practice runs only while the provider is stopped, because H2 file access here is one Java process at a time.

    4.4 Store a book and read it in a fresh program

    Read/type Book first, then BookStore. JPA names the Java entity Book, while SQL storage uses library_books. The Java attribute title maps to book_title; JPQL must still use b.title.

    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/Book.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import jakarta.persistence.Column;
import jakarta.persistence.Entity;
import jakarta.persistence.GeneratedValue;
import jakarta.persistence.GenerationType;
import jakarta.persistence.Id;
import jakarta.persistence.Table;

@Entity
@Table(name = "library_books")
public class Book {
    @Id
    @GeneratedValue(strategy = GenerationType.IDENTITY)
    @Column(name = "book_id")
    private Long id;

    @Column(name = "book_title", nullable = false, length = 80)
    private String title;

    public Book() {
    }

    public Book(String title) {
        setTitle(title);
    }

    public Long getId() {
        return id;
    }

    public String getTitle() {
        return title;
    }

    public void setTitle(String title) {
        if (title == null || title.isBlank() || title.trim().length() > 80) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Title must contain 1 to 80 characters after trimming.");
        }
        this.title = title.trim();
    }
}
    Book codeExplain it before continuing
    @Entity / @TableBook is the Java entity name; library_books is its database table. An annotation supplies mapping information to Hibernate.
    @Id / @GeneratedValue / Long idid is the primary key. Long can hold null before insertion; the database supplies an identity value. Because @Id is on the field, Hibernate reads/writes the fields directly; an id setter is not required here.
    Book() / Book(String title)The no-argument constructor lets Hibernate create an object while loading. Your title constructor calls the validation setter before a new book is saved.
    title == null || title.isBlank() || ...Reject missing text, whitespace-only text or text over the trimmed limit. Java stops checking OR once a condition is true, so null never reaches isBlank. trim removes ordinary spaces at either end; spaces between words remain.
    this.title = title.trim()The parameter title is this call's input. this.title is the field of this Book object. Store the cleaned input there. The title-length rule in this exercise uses Java String.length; practise with ordinary letters and spaces.
    @Column / gettersThe column mapping sets its storage name, size and non-null requirement. The Java setter gives a helpful input error before insertion. Getters let other Java code, and the JSON writer, read the values.

    Pause and predict: for new Book(" Java Foundations "), which constructor runs, what does title become, and when is id assigned? For new Book(" "), does the call reach a database transaction? Explain before reading BookStore.

    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/BookStore.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import java.util.List;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityManager;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityManagerFactory;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityTransaction;

public class BookStore {
    private final EntityManagerFactory factory;

    public BookStore(EntityManagerFactory factory) {
        this.factory = factory;
    }

    public List<Book> list(String title) {
        EntityManager em = factory.createEntityManager();
        try {
            if (title == null) {
                return em.createQuery("SELECT b FROM Book b ORDER BY b.id", Book.class).getResultList();
            }
            return em.createQuery("SELECT b FROM Book b WHERE b.title = :title ORDER BY b.id", Book.class)
                .setParameter("title", title).getResultList();
        } finally {
            em.close();
        }
    }

    public Book find(long id) {
        EntityManager em = factory.createEntityManager();
        try {
            return em.find(Book.class, id);
        } finally {
            em.close();
        }
    }

    public Book add(String title) {
        Book book = new Book(title);
        EntityManager em = factory.createEntityManager();
        EntityTransaction tx = em.getTransaction();
        try {
            tx.begin();
            em.persist(book);
            tx.commit();
            return book;
        } catch (RuntimeException problem) {
            if (tx.isActive()) tx.rollback();
            throw problem;
        } finally {
            em.close();
        }
    }
}
    Step in addWhat it proves
    new Book(title)Construct and validate a plain object; id is initially null.
    createEntityManager / tx.beginOpen this operation’s context and transaction.
    persist / commitTrack the new entity and keep its insert. The database generates an id; commit must succeed before add returns.
    catch / rollback / finally closeCancel an active failed transaction, report the failure and close the working context.
    list(title) with setParameterKeep query text separate from the requested exact title. An empty list is a valid no-match result.

    The factory can be shared by request handlers; each store call creates and closes its own EntityManager. The returned Book has simple id/title fields. Changing this detached object later would not automatically write the database. Revisit 1.8B for managed updates, flush, rollback and deletion.

    Read the small console launcher
    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/DatabasePractice.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import java.util.List;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityManagerFactory;

public class DatabasePractice {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply one quoted title to add, or no arguments to list.");
        EntityManagerFactory factory = LibraryDatabase.open();
        try {
            BookStore store = new BookStore(factory);
            if (args.length == 1) {
                Book saved = store.add(args[0]);
                System.out.println("Saved book " + saved.getId() + ": " + saved.getTitle());
            } else {
                List<Book> books = store.list(null);
                for (Book book : books) {
                    System.out.println(book.getId() + ": " + book.getTitle());
                }
                System.out.println("Books: " + books.size());
            }
        } finally {
            factory.close();
        }
    }
}

    With one quoted argument it adds that title; with no arguments it lists. List(null) selects all rows. The launcher closes the factory in finally. It is setup you may reuse; write and explain the mapping/store yourself for independent work.

    java -cp target/session4-practice-1.0.jar com.aptech.practice.DatabasePractice

    A fresh personal database prints Books: 0. If you already have rows, record the initial state and printed database path.

    java -cp target/session4-practice-1.0.jar com.aptech.practice.DatabasePractice " Java Foundations "

    Expected for a fresh file: Saved book 1: Java Foundations. Record your actual id. The title is trimmed, and generated ids need not equal the number of rows.

    java -cp target/session4-practice-1.0.jar com.aptech.practice.DatabasePractice

    The new Java process must list the same id/title. Provider startup can take tens of seconds; wait for the final application output and record the last log if it appears stalled. Do not start a second process against the same file.

    4.5 Build the REST side and test rejected requests

    Read/type the resource. Compare it with 2.13B: the domain changed to books; the request → validate → store → commit → response flow remains.

    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/BookResource.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import java.util.List;
import jakarta.ws.rs.Consumes;
import jakarta.ws.rs.GET;
import jakarta.ws.rs.POST;
import jakarta.ws.rs.Path;
import jakarta.ws.rs.PathParam;
import jakarta.ws.rs.Produces;
import jakarta.ws.rs.QueryParam;
import jakarta.ws.rs.core.Context;
import jakarta.ws.rs.core.MediaType;
import jakarta.ws.rs.core.Response;
import jakarta.ws.rs.core.UriInfo;

@Path("/books")
@Produces(MediaType.APPLICATION_JSON)
public class BookResource {
    private final BookStore store;

    public BookResource(BookStore store) {
        this.store = store;
    }

    @GET
    public List<Book> list(@QueryParam("title") String title) {
        return store.list(title);
    }

    @GET
    @Path("/{id}")
    public Response get(@PathParam("id") long id) {
        if (id < 1) return Response.status(400).entity(new Error("Book id must be positive.")).build();
        Book book = store.find(id);
        if (book == null) return Response.status(404).entity(new Error("Book not found.")).build();
        return Response.ok(book).build();
    }

    @POST
    @Consumes(MediaType.APPLICATION_JSON)
    public Response create(Input input, @Context UriInfo uriInfo) {
        try {
            Book saved = store.add(input == null ? null : input.title);
            return Response.created(uriInfo.getAbsolutePathBuilder()
                .path(String.valueOf(saved.getId())).build()).entity(saved).build();
        } catch (IllegalArgumentException problem) {
            return Response.status(400).entity(new Error(problem.getMessage())).build();
        }
    }

    public static class Input {
        public String title;
    }

    public static class Error {
        public String message;

        public Error(String message) {
            this.message = message;
        }
    }
}

    Input is a nested class for the JSON title. The entity's getters expose id/title in the JSON response. The resource validates an id before lookup, translates not found to 404, and translates the title's IllegalArgumentException to 400. Successful POST builds Location from the request URL and actual generated id after add commits.

    Read the staged provider launcher
    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/PracticeServer.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpServer;
import java.net.URI;
import java.util.concurrent.ExecutorService;
import java.util.concurrent.Executors;
import jakarta.persistence.EntityManagerFactory;
import jakarta.xml.ws.Endpoint;
import org.glassfish.jersey.jackson.JacksonFeature;
import org.glassfish.jersey.jdkhttp.JdkHttpServerFactory;
import org.glassfish.jersey.server.ResourceConfig;
import org.glassfish.jersey.server.ServerProperties;

public class PracticeServer {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        boolean restOnly = args.length == 1 && "--rest-only".equals(args[0]);
        if (args.length > 1 || (args.length == 1 && !restOnly)) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Use no arguments, or --rest-only.");
        }
        EntityManagerFactory factory = LibraryDatabase.open();
        ExecutorService requests = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(4);
        HttpServer rest = null;
        Endpoint soap = null;
        try {
            BookStore store = new BookStore(factory);
            ResourceConfig config = new ResourceConfig();
            config.property(ServerProperties.WADL_FEATURE_DISABLE, true);
            config.register(new BookResource(store));
            config.register(JacksonFeature.class);
            int restPort = Integer.getInteger("practice.rest.port", 8098);
            int soapPort = Integer.getInteger("practice.soap.port", 8099);
            String base = "http://127.0.0.1:" + restPort + "/api/";
            rest = JdkHttpServerFactory.createHttpServer(URI.create(base), config, false);
            rest.setExecutor(requests);
            rest.start();
            if (!restOnly) {
                soap = Endpoint.create(new LibraryServiceImpl(store));
                soap.setExecutor(requests);
                soap.publish("http://127.0.0.1:" + soapPort + "/library");
                System.out.println("SOAP: http://127.0.0.1:" + soapPort + "/library?wsdl");
            }
            System.out.println("REST: " + base + "books");
            System.out.println("Press Enter here to stop safely.");
            System.in.read();
        } finally {
            if (soap != null) soap.stop();
            if (rest != null) rest.stop(0);
            requests.shutdownNow();
            factory.close();
        }
    }
}

    It opens one factory and BookStore. Jersey registers a BookResource instance with that store. In full mode Metro receives a LibraryServiceImpl using the same store. --rest-only skips SOAP while you practise REST. The explicit executor provides worker threads to the servers; cleanup stops endpoints, shuts down workers and closes the factory. Press Enter in A after your requests finish to stop safely.

    Start just REST in A

    java --add-modules jdk.httpserver -jar target/session4-practice-1.0.jar --rest-only

    Wait for REST: http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books. SOAP is intentionally absent in this stage. In B enter your copy of session4-practice so the request files are found.

    curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books
    session4-practice/new-book.json
    {"title":"Java Foundations"}
    curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books -H "Content-Type: application/json" --data-binary "@new-book.json"

    Expect 201, id/title and Location. Adding the same title twice is allowed; title is not the primary key. Record the actual newly returned id and GET the complete Location. Example only, if it ends in /2:

    curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books/2

    Replace 2 with your actual id. Expect 200 and that book. Then test the exact-title query:

    curl.exe -i "http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books?title=Java%20Foundations"

    %20 represents a space in the URL. This is an exact, case-sensitive match; names are bound as values, not joined into JPQL.

    session4-practice/blank-book.json
    {"title":"   "}
    curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8098/api/books -H "Content-Type: application/json" --data-binary "@blank-book.json"

    Expect 400 and no added row. Record the collection before and after. Create your own file with a title of 80 characters, then 81; accept the former and reject the latter. Also test JSON null, an absent id and id zero.

    CaseExpected
    Valid trimmed title; lengths 1 and 80201, stored trimmed value, actual Location.
    Blank, absent/null or 81-character title400; collection unchanged.
    GET absent positive id / GET id 0404 / 400.
    Malformed JSON / unsupported text/plain POST400 / 415.
    Exact title with no match200 and [].

    4.6 Add SOAP and use the same stored book

    Now read/type the interface and implementation. Keep names and roles clear: the interface is the operation agreement; the implementation supplies the rule or store lookup. It receives the existing BookStore through its constructor.

    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/LibraryService.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import jakarta.jws.WebMethod;
import jakarta.jws.WebParam;
import jakarta.jws.WebResult;
import jakarta.jws.WebService;
import jakarta.jws.soap.SOAPBinding;

@WebService(name = "LibraryService", targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session4")
@SOAPBinding(style = SOAPBinding.Style.DOCUMENT, use = SOAPBinding.Use.LITERAL,
    parameterStyle = SOAPBinding.ParameterStyle.WRAPPED)
public interface LibraryService {
    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "hours")
    String getLibraryHours();

    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "title")
    String getBookTitle(@WebParam(name = "bookId") long bookId) throws LibraryFault;

    @WebMethod
    @WebResult(name = "chargeNGN")
    int calculatePrintCharge(@WebParam(name = "pages") int pages) throws LibraryFault;
}
    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/LibraryServiceImpl.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import jakarta.jws.WebService;

@WebService(endpointInterface = "com.aptech.practice.LibraryService",
    targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session4", serviceName = "AptechLibraryService",
    portName = "LibraryPort")
public class LibraryServiceImpl implements LibraryService {
    private final BookStore store;

    public LibraryServiceImpl(BookStore store) {
        this.store = store;
    }

    @Override
    public String getLibraryHours() {
        return "Mon-Fri 09:00-17:00";
    }

    @Override
    public String getBookTitle(long bookId) throws LibraryFault {
        if (bookId < 1) throw new LibraryFault("Book id must be positive.");
        Book book = store.find(bookId);
        if (book == null) throw new LibraryFault("Book not found.");
        return book.getTitle();
    }

    @Override
    public int calculatePrintCharge(int pages) throws LibraryFault {
        if (pages < 1 || pages > 20) throw new LibraryFault("Pages must be between 1 and 20.");
        return pages * 10;
    }
}

    getLibraryHours returns text without an input. getBookTitle uses a long identifier and returns text read from the database. calculatePrintCharge takes an int and returns an int. The last two declare a LibraryFault for expected application rejections. Database connection failures are unexpected runtime failures, distinct from our declared not-found/validation fault.

    The declared fault: match the pattern from Session 3
    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/LibraryFaultInfo.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlType;

@XmlType(name = "LibraryFaultInfo", namespace = "urn:aptech:session4")
public class LibraryFaultInfo {
    private String reason;

    public LibraryFaultInfo() {
    }

    public LibraryFaultInfo(String reason) {
        this.reason = reason;
    }

    public String getReason() {
        return reason;
    }

    public void setReason(String reason) {
        this.reason = reason;
    }
}
    session4-practice/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/LibraryFault.java
    package com.aptech.practice;

import jakarta.xml.ws.WebFault;

@WebFault(name = "LibraryFault", targetNamespace = "urn:aptech:session4",
    faultBean = "com.aptech.practice.LibraryFaultInfo")
public class LibraryFault extends Exception {
    private final LibraryFaultInfo faultInfo;

    public LibraryFault(String message) {
        this(message, new LibraryFaultInfo(message));
    }

    public LibraryFault(String message, LibraryFaultInfo faultInfo) {
        super(message);
        this.faultInfo = faultInfo;
    }

    public LibraryFault(String message, LibraryFaultInfo faultInfo, Throwable cause) {
        super(message, cause);
        this.faultInfo = faultInfo;
    }

    public LibraryFaultInfo getFaultInfo() {
        return faultInfo;
    }
}

    The detail bean carries reason. WebFault declares its namespace/name. The constructors let the implementation create a fault and compatible Java clients reconstruct it with message/detail/cause. throws in the interface declares a possible checked failure; throw in a method actually raises it.

    Stop REST-only, then start full mode in A

    Press Enter in A and wait for the prompt. If you edited sources, save and run mvn package before restarting. Then run:

    java --add-modules jdk.httpserver -jar target/session4-practice-1.0.jar

    Wait for both REST and SOAP startup lines. Open http://127.0.0.1:8099/library?wsdl. Find service AptechLibraryService, port LibraryPort, target namespace urn:aptech:session4, its operations and imported XSD. Read the schema's actual input names and order; getBookTitle's bookId is xs:long and calculatePrintCharge's pages is xs:int.

    Send a no-input operation in B

    Reuse the header-file method from Session 3. This file contains HTTP headers, not XML. The two literal quotation marks after SOAPAction send the empty action value without Windows-shell quoting problems.

    session4-practice/soap-headers.txt

SOAPAction: ""
    session4-practice/hours.xml

</soap:Envelope>

    Expect HTTP 200 and hours Mon-Fri 09:00-17:00. A no-input operation still needs its correctly namespaced wrapper inside the SOAP Body.

    Read a book saved through REST

    session4-practice/book-title.xml
    <soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/" xmlns:l="urn:aptech:session4">
    <soap:Body><l:getBookTitle><bookId>1</bookId></l:getBookTitle></soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>

    Before sending: replace the example bookId 1 in your request file with the actual id you recorded from REST. Keep the operation namespace and the unqualified bookId child as shown by this contract. An identifier copied from another learner's database might be absent in yours.

    Expect 200 and the title of that stored row. Compare the SOAP title with GET at the REST Location. The messages are different representations; the stored record is the same. Neither service passes a live Java object to the remote client.

    Test arithmetic and a declared fault

    session4-practice/print-charge.xml
    <soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://schemas.xmlsoap.org/soap/envelope/" xmlns:l="urn:aptech:session4">
    <soap:Body><l:calculatePrintCharge><pages>3</pages></l:calculatePrintCharge></soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>

    <soap:Body><l:calculatePrintCharge><pages>0</pages></l:calculatePrintCharge></soap:Body>
</soap:Envelope>
    curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8099/library -H "@soap-headers.txt" --data-binary "@invalid-print.xml"

    Expected: 3 pages → 30 in chargeNGN with HTTP 200; 0 pages → HTTP 500 SOAP Fault with reason “Pages must be between 1 and 20.” Test 1, 20 and 21 as well. A zero value fits xs:int but violates the business rule. SOAP Header is optional; the HTTP SOAPAction header is a separate message layer.
