    4.7 Build a separate client from the contract

    Keep the full provider running in A. The separate generated-client project has Metro’s runtime and wsimport plugin. It does not depend on the provider's implementation or Hibernate. Your handwritten code chooses calls; the generated types implement the published wire agreement.

    Set-Location generated-client
    mvn package
    Get-ChildItem target/generated-sources/wsimport/com/aptech/practice/generated

    These commands begin with B in session4-practice. Wait for BUILD SUCCESS. Find AptechLibraryService.java, LibraryService.java and the generated fault/data classes. The WSDL URL defaults to http://127.0.0.1:8099/library?wsdl.

    Read this client project's pom
    session4-practice/generated-client/pom.xml
    <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<project xmlns="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0" xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
    xsi:schemaLocation="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0 https://maven.apache.org/xsd/maven-4.0.0.xsd">
    <modelVersion>4.0.0</modelVersion>
    <groupId>com.aptech</groupId><artifactId>session4-generated-client</artifactId><version>1.0</version>
    <properties>
        <maven.compiler.release>17</maven.compiler.release>
        <project.build.sourceEncoding>UTF-8</project.build.sourceEncoding>
        <library.wsdl>http://127.0.0.1:8099/library?wsdl</library.wsdl>
    </properties>
    <dependencies><dependency><groupId>com.sun.xml.ws</groupId><artifactId>jaxws-rt</artifactId><version>4.0.3</version></dependency></dependencies>
    <build><plugins>
        <plugin><groupId>com.sun.xml.ws</groupId><artifactId>jaxws-maven-plugin</artifactId><version>4.0.3</version>
            <executions><execution><goals><goal>wsimport</goal></goals></execution></executions>
            <configuration>
                <wsdlUrls><wsdlUrl>${library.wsdl}</wsdlUrl></wsdlUrls>
                <packageName>com.aptech.practice.generated</packageName>
                <keep>true</keep><xnocompile>true</xnocompile>
            </configuration>
        </plugin>
        <plugin><groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId><artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId><version>3.13.0</version></plugin>
        <plugin><groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId><artifactId>maven-shade-plugin</artifactId><version>3.6.0</version>
            <executions><execution><phase>package</phase><goals><goal>shade</goal></goals><configuration>
                <createDependencyReducedPom>false</createDependencyReducedPom>
                <filters><filter><artifact>*:*</artifact><excludes>
                    <exclude>META-INF/*.SF</exclude><exclude>META-INF/*.RSA</exclude><exclude>META-INF/*.DSA</exclude><exclude>module-info.class</exclude>
                </excludes></filter></filters>
                <transformers>
                    <transformer implementation="org.apache.maven.plugins.shade.resource.ServicesResourceTransformer"/>
                    <transformer implementation="org.apache.maven.plugins.shade.resource.ManifestResourceTransformer"><mainClass>com.aptech.practice.client.GeneratedLibraryClient</mainClass></transformer>
                </transformers>
            </configuration></execution></executions>
        </plugin>
    </plugins></build>
</project>

    library.wsdl supplies the description URL; packageName selects the generated package. wsimport is standard code generation, not AI. Keep your handwritten source outside target/generated-sources.

    session4-practice/generated-client/src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/client/GeneratedLibraryClient.java
    package com.aptech.practice.client;

import java.net.URI;
import com.aptech.practice.generated.AptechLibraryService;
import com.aptech.practice.generated.LibraryService;

public class GeneratedLibraryClient {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length != 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply the actual book id returned by REST or the console.");
        long bookId = Long.parseLong(args[0]);
        int port = Integer.getInteger("practice.soap.port", 8099);
        AptechLibraryService service = new AptechLibraryService(
            URI.create("http://127.0.0.1:" + port + "/library?wsdl").toURL());
        LibraryService proxy = service.getLibraryPort();
        System.out.println("Hours: " + proxy.getLibraryHours());
        System.out.println("Book " + bookId + ": " + proxy.getBookTitle(bookId));
        System.out.println("3 pages: " + proxy.calculatePrintCharge(3) + " NGN");
    }
}
    java -jar target/session4-generated-client-1.0.jar 1

    Replace the final example 1 with your actual REST-created id before running. Expect Hours: Mon-Fri 09:00-17:00; Book {actual id}: {stored title}; 3 pages: 30 NGN. A wrong id causes the declared remote fault; a stopped server causes a transport failure. This simple main reports unhandled errors; explain the first useful reason rather than assuming every failure is the same.

    If an operation/type changed, build/restart A, verify its current WSDL and run mvn clean package in generated-client. Read generated files but do not patch them by hand. A changed stored title does not change the schema or require regeneration. Return B to the server project afterward:

    Test sequenceAcceptance evidence
    Console add, close and list in a new processSame id/title survive program closure.
    REST create a distinct title and follow its actual Location201, Location GET 200, expected id/title.

    Unseen small change + next-day recallWrite, test and explain a new variation with completed solutions closed.

    Prove interoperability only as far as your actual evidence: raw SOAP and a generated Java client call the same contract. A partner using another language must match the schema, binding, endpoint and security requirements and be tested with their actual toolkit. Loopback addresses refer to the caller's own computer.

      4.10 Build your own equipment catalogue

      Independent assignment: a centre tracks fictional equipment items. Design a separate service in your personal copy, using the dependencies and learned setup. Do not submit the reference library renamed with no new behaviour.

      PartRequirement
      Entity/storeItem with generated id and name; table equipment_items, columns item_id/item_name. Trim names, allow 1–60 characters, persist and query by exact name with a bound value.
      RESTGET /api/items, GET /api/items/{id}, POST name JSON; 201/Location after commit, 400 validation/invalid id, 404 absent positive id.
      SOAPString getItemName(long itemId); int calculateLoanCharge(int days). Allow 0–14 days at 25 illustrative NGN per day; reject other counts with your declared EquipmentFault.
      AddressesREST http://127.0.0.1:8090/api/items; SOAP http://127.0.0.1:8091/equipment; namespace urn:aptech:equipment.
      ClientA separate WSDL-generated client that calls the actual saved item id and a valid loan-charge operation.
      PersistenceSame saved item read from REST/SOAP before and after server restart.

      Where to implement it

      Create src/main/java/com/aptech/practice/equipment with package com.aptech.practice.equipment. EquipmentServer should open the same persistence unit using its own database base ./data/equipment, create one ItemStore, pass it to both interfaces and clean up both endpoints and workers.

      Reuse the library build configuration. Your manifest still points to PracticeServer, so run your new server by its fully qualified class name after writing/building it:

      java --add-modules jdk.httpserver -cp target/session4-practice-1.0.jar com.aptech.practice.equipment.EquipmentServer

      For the separate client, create an equipment-client folder using the generated-client pom as setup reference. Set its WSDL URL to your equipment endpoint, choose a separate generated package and update the manifest main class to your handwritten EquipmentClient. Write that client against names actually generated from your chosen WSDL service/port. Do not leave the Library imports/main class while pointing at the equipment contract.

      Small hints after a serious attempt

      SESSION 5 · JAXB

      Turn Java objects into XML, and XML into usable data

      5.0 Understand the data inside a service message

      In Sessions 3 and 4, Metro turned Java arguments and results into SOAP XML. Now you will learn the object-to-XML part directly. JAXB, historically Java Architecture for XML Binding and now Jakarta XML Binding, maps Java data to XML and back. It does not publish a web service by itself.

      What the August 2024 manual requires

      WEBSRV–TL5 covers book Session 5: explain JAXB; marshalling/unmarshalling; XML schemas; binding best practices; compare JAX-RS, JAX-WS and JAXB; assess their advantages and disadvantages. This lesson covers those topics with a fictional student record and an independent loan-ticket assignment.

      Before you beginDemonstrate itRevisit
      Java objectsCreate an object; explain a constructor, getter, setter and this.field.1.2, 2.2
      XMLIdentify root, child, attribute, namespace and escaped text.3.5, 3.6
      Build/runUse the correct project folder, build a JAR and run a named class.4.3

      Work in a personal copy of session5-jaxb. Read → predict → type → run → change → close the example → attempt a similar task without AI. Your notes, build configuration, compiler and ordinary schema-generation tools are allowed. A working copy alone is not evidence that you can explain or change it.

      Instructor: core lesson and follow-up time

      After setup, allow 15 minutes for recall, 20 for mapping, 20 for marshalling, 20 for reading/round-trip, 25 for schema validation, 10 for comparison and 10 for a small independent change. That is a two-hour core. Reserve a further 60–90-minute lab for schema generation, 2–3 hours for the assignment and a 20–30-minute next-day recall check. Split long periods into 45–60-minute blocks. These are starting estimates; demonstrated readiness decides progression.

      5.1 An object and XML text are different things

      A Java object has fields and methods in the running JVM. An XML document is text with names and values. The text <full_name>Amina</full_name> cannot call getFullName. JAXB creates or reads the corresponding Java values so application code can use methods.

      session5-jaxb/beginner/PlainStudentPractice.java
      public class PlainStudentPractice {
    static class Card {
        private String fullName;
        Card(String fullName) { this.fullName = fullName; }
        String getFullName() { return fullName; }
    }
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        Card card = new Card("Amina & Tunde");
        String xml = "<student><full_name>Amina &amp; Tunde</full_name></student>";
        System.out.println("Object getter: " + card.getFullName());
        System.out.println("XML text: " + xml);
        System.out.println("Same object as another new Card: " + (card == new Card("Amina & Tunde")));
    }
}
      Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session5-jaxb"
      javac beginner/PlainStudentPractice.java
      java -cp beginner PlainStudentPractice
      Expected Java warm-up output
      Object getter: Amina & Tunde
XML text: <student><full_name>Amina &amp; Tunde</full_name></student>
Same object as another new Card: false

      This JDK-only example places an object and a fixed XML string beside each other; it does not convert them. Each new Card creates another object. == compares object identity; later we compare the data using getters.

      Word/codeMeaning
      MarshallingJava object → XML representation. Think of writing the card values into a document.
      UnmarshallingXML representation → a new Java object. Read the document into the mapped fields.
      Student.classThe Java Class description of the type to map; it is not a Student instance.
      JAXBContextA prepared description of the classes and their XML mapping.
      Marshaller / UnmarshallerWorkers created from the context to write XML / read XML.
      (Student) reader.unmarshal(...)A cast: treat the returned Object as Student. It succeeds only when the mapped result is that type.
      StringWriter / StringReaderWrite text in memory / read a known String as input; neither is a network service.
      YOU CHANGE, THEN YOU DO

      Change the fictional name in both representations. Predict the result if the name includes &. Then, with the example closed, create your own two-field equipment card and explain which value is an object and which is XML text. Do not attempt string-based XML serialization as your final solution.

      5.2 Read one XML record before mapping it

      session5-jaxb/examples/valid-student.xml
      <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<student id="101">
    <full_name>Amina &amp; Tunde</full_name>
    <email>learner@example.com</email>
    <course>ADSE</course>
    <gpa>3.5</gpa>
</student>
      PartWhat to identify
      studentThe root element enclosing this one record. This file is not a SOAP Envelope.
      id="101"An attribute of student. It is not an id child element.
      full_name, email, course, gpaThe child elements and their values, in the agreed sequence.
      Amina &amp; Tunde in XMLThe &amp; escape represents one literal & in the Java String. JAXB writes/reads the escape for you.
      No target namespaceThis first lab uses unqualified element names. Adding xmlns="urn:other" changes their names and breaks this mapping/schema.

      Well-formed means XML syntax is correct: tags close, there is one root and special characters are escaped. Schema-valid means the document also follows the selected XSD structure and restrictions. Application-valid means it satisfies your program’s rules, such as a name not being spaces only. These checks answer different questions.

      PREDICT BEFORE RUNNING

      For each change, say which check should fail: omit a closing tag; remove course; change gpa to 4.1; use a spaces-only name; add a different default namespace. Keep these predictions for 5.9. Use copies of the request files so the valid reference stays available.

      5.3 Build the standalone JAXB lab

      Use JDK 17+ and Maven from 2.3. Commands below assume your session5-jaxb project folder. There is no server to leave running in this lesson: each demo runs, prints and exits. Files are local; no database operation occurs.

      java -version
      javac -version
      mvn -version
      mvn package

      Wait for BUILD SUCCESS. JDK 17+ does not bundle JAXB; the Maven build supplies Jakarta XML Binding API 4.0.1 and the JAXB implementation 4.0.4, then includes their runtime libraries in the JAR. The first build may need an internet connection to download dependencies. An offline build works only with a complete cache.

      LocationPurpose

      Build configuration to reuse

      The compiler targets Java 17. The shade plugin includes runtime dependencies and their service-provider descriptions. The manifest selects JaxbMarshallingDemo for -jar; -cp plus a fully qualified class name runs another demo. This is build setup you may reuse.

      Save source changes, rebuild, then rerun. Editing an XML input file needs no Java rebuild. Maven clean removes target output; download/save your evidence elsewhere before cleaning. If you move the project, use its current folder and verify the printed absolute output path.

      5.4 Tell JAXB which properties belong in XML

      Read/type Student. It is a plain mapped data class, without JPA annotations. Its JAXB mapping describes XML; it does not save the student to a database. The password value is fictional and demonstrates an excluded property.

      session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/Student.java
      package com.aptech.jaxb;
import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.*;

@XmlRootElement(name = "student")
@XmlAccessorType(XmlAccessType.NONE)
@XmlType(propOrder = {"fullName", "email", "course", "gpa"})
public class Student {
    private int id;
    private String fullName;
    private String email;
    private String course;
    private double gpa;
    private String password; // Fictional demo value only.

    public Student() { }
    public Student(int id, String fullName, String email, String course, double gpa, String password) {
        this.id = id; this.fullName = fullName; this.email = email;
        this.course = course; this.gpa = gpa; this.password = password;
    }
    @XmlAttribute(name = "id", required = true)
    public int getId() { return id; }
    public void setId(int id) { this.id = id; }
    @XmlElement(name = "full_name", required = true)
    public String getFullName() { return fullName; }
    public void setFullName(String fullName) { this.fullName = fullName; }
    @XmlElement(name = "email", required = true)
    public String getEmail() { return email; }
    public void setEmail(String email) { this.email = email; }
    @XmlElement(name = "course", required = true)
    public String getCourse() { return course; }
    public void setCourse(String course) { this.course = course; }
    @XmlElement(name = "gpa", required = true)
    public double getGpa() { return gpa; }
    public void setGpa(double gpa) { this.gpa = gpa; }
    @XmlTransient
    public String getPassword() { return password; }
    public void setPassword(String password) { this.password = password; }
    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "Student [id=" + id + ", fullName=" + fullName + ", email=" + email
            + ", course=" + course + ", gpa=" + gpa + "]";
    }
}
      CodeExplain its job
      import ...annotation.*Import annotation types from that package. The star is a Java import wildcard, not an XML rule.
      @XmlRootElement(name="student")Use student as the outer element name for this class.
      @XmlAccessorType(XmlAccessType.NONE)Only explicitly annotated properties/fields are bound. Here the annotations are consistently on getters.
      @XmlAttribute on getIdPut the id value in an attribute; a corresponding setter lets JAXB fill it when reading.
      @XmlElement(name="full_name")Map Java property fullName to XML element full_name. The two names need not be spelled the same.
      @XmlType(propOrder={"fullName", ...})List Java property names in child-element order. Do not put full_name, id or password in this array.
      required=trueDescribes required content in schema generation. It does not by itself enable runtime schema validation.
      public Student()A no-argument constructor lets JAXB create this ordinary mapped object while reading.
      Student(...six inputs...)Your convenience constructor assigns one object’s initial data. JAXB does not need to call this constructor to read XML.
      this.fullName = fullNameThe left side is this object’s field; the right side is the constructor/setter parameter.
      @XmlTransient on getPasswordExclude this property from JAXB XML. It is not encryption, access control or a rule for every other serializer.
      toStringA useful description for the terminal; this method intentionally excludes the password too. It is not XML serialization.

      With NONE, unannotated properties already stay out. XmlTransient makes the exclusion intent explicit and also helps explain the pattern used with other access strategies. Avoid marking both a field and its getter as the same XML property: that can produce duplicate mapping errors. The generated XJC model later uses FIELD access consistently instead.

      WE DO → ONE CONTROLLED CHANGE

      In your copy change full_name’s XML name to display_name. Predict the marshalled tag, save/build/run and inspect it. Explain why propOrder still uses fullName. Restore full_name before reading the supplied student schema. A mapping-name change must agree with input documents and the acceptance schema.

      5.5 Marshal an object to text and a real file

      session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/JaxbMarshallingDemo.java
      package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.io.StringWriter;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Marshaller;

public class JaxbMarshallingDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply an optional output XML path.");
        Path output = Path.of(args.length == 1 ? args[0] : "target/student_output.xml");
        Files.createDirectories(output.toAbsolutePath().getParent());
        Student student = new Student(101, "Amina & Tunde", "learner@example.com", "ADSE", 3.5, "DEMO_ONLY");
        StudentRules.check(student);
        JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
        Marshaller writer = context.createMarshaller();
        writer.setProperty(Marshaller.JAXB_FORMATTED_OUTPUT, true);
        writer.setProperty(Marshaller.JAXB_ENCODING, "UTF-8");
        StringWriter text = new StringWriter();
        writer.marshal(student, text);
        System.out.println(text);
        writer.marshal(student, output.toFile());
        System.out.println("Saved XML: " + output.toAbsolutePath().normalize());
    }
}
      java -jar target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar
      Get-Content target/student_output.xml
      StepWhat happens
      new Student / StudentRules.checkCreate a fictional object, then check its application rules. No file has been written yet.
      JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class)Prepare the mapping for this class. Reuse this context for both writes in this process.
      context.createMarshallerCreate a conversion worker. It writes XML according to the prepared mapping.
      JAXB_FORMATTED_OUTPUT / JAXB_ENCODINGReadable indentation and UTF-8 encoding; formatting does not change the values.
      marshal(student, text)Write XML into the StringWriter in memory, then print the text.
      marshal(student, output.toFile())Write an actual XML file; the printed absolute path identifies it.
      Files.createDirectoriesCreate the destination’s parent folders if needed, before writing.

      Expect the same root, attribute and child values as the valid document in 5.2. The declaration may include standalone="yes" and indentation may vary. Amina & Tunde becomes Amina &amp; Tunde in XML; DEMO_ONLY/password is absent. Compare the parsed values, not an exact text snapshot.

      Change id to 103, name to Ada <Coder>, course to DISM and GPA to 4.0. Save/build/run. Predict &lt; and &gt; in the XML and explain why the getter will later return ordinary angle brackets. Restore the reference values afterward.

      5.6 Read XML into a new object

      session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/JaxbUnmarshallingDemo.java
      package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;

public class JaxbUnmarshallingDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply an optional input XML path.");
        Path input = Path.of(args.length == 1 ? args[0] : "examples/valid-student.xml");
        JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
        Unmarshaller reader = context.createUnmarshaller();
        // No XSD is attached in this comparison demo. It is not acceptance validation.
        Student student = SecureXml.read(reader, input);
        System.out.println("Mapped without XSD validation: " + student);
        System.out.println("Full name getter: " + student.getFullName());
        System.out.println("Password omitted from XML; getter is null: " + (student.getPassword() == null));
    }
}
      java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.JaxbUnmarshallingDemo
      Expected unmarshal output
      Mapped without XSD validation: Student [id=101, fullName=Amina & Tunde, email=learner@example.com, course=ADSE, gpa=3.5]
Full name getter: Amina & Tunde
Password omitted from XML; getter is null: true

      SecureXml supplies the parser setup; you can reuse it. It reads the file and returns the mapped Student. JAXB creates the object with its no-argument constructor and fills the annotated properties. Calling getFullName demonstrates that the result is a Java object. &amp; was decoded to &; the excluded password was never restored, so that reference remains null.

      This first reader attaches no XSD and checks no StudentRules. It demonstrates mapping only. Correct XML syntax and recognised mapping do not prove that the input is acceptable for your application. The next command deliberately reads a missing-course file:

      java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.JaxbUnmarshallingDemo examples/missing-course.xml

      Expected: the object is mapped with course=null. Even with required=true on the getter, this reader has not enabled runtime schema validation. Do not treat this result as an accepted student record. Section 5.9 attaches the chosen XSD and checks the application rules before printing ACCEPTED.

      YOU TRY • USE THE VALUES

      5.7 Prove a round-trip preserves the agreed data

      A round-trip means object → XML file → a new object. It should preserve the mapped values for our test. It cannot recover a deliberately excluded property, and it does not restore the original object identity.

      session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/RoundTripDemo.java
      package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Marshaller;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;
import javax.xml.validation.Schema;

public class RoundTripDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply an optional output XML path.");
        Path file = Path.of(args.length == 1 ? args[0] : "target/roundtrip.xml");
        Files.createDirectories(file.toAbsolutePath().getParent());
        JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
        Schema schema = SecureXml.schema(Path.of("student_schema.xsd"));
        Student before = new Student(102, "Ada <Coder>", "ada@example.com", "DISM", 4.0, "DEMO_ONLY");
        StudentRules.check(before);
        Marshaller writer = context.createMarshaller();
        writer.setSchema(schema);
        writer.setProperty(Marshaller.JAXB_FORMATTED_OUTPUT, true);
        writer.marshal(before, file.toFile());
        Unmarshaller reader = context.createUnmarshaller();
        reader.setSchema(schema);
        reader.setEventHandler(event -> false);
        Student after = SecureXml.read(reader, file);
        StudentRules.check(after);
        boolean sameValues = before.getId() == after.getId()
            && before.getFullName().equals(after.getFullName())
            && before.getEmail().equals(after.getEmail())
            && before.getCourse().equals(after.getCourse())
            && Double.compare(before.getGpa(), after.getGpa()) == 0;
        System.out.println("Mapped values preserved: " + sameValues);
        System.out.println("Same Java object: " + (before == after));
        System.out.println("Excluded password after read is null: " + (after.getPassword() == null));
        System.out.println("Saved XML: " + file.toAbsolutePath().normalize());
        if (!sameValues || before == after || after.getPassword() != null)
            throw new IllegalStateException("Round-trip expectations failed.");
    }
}
      java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.RoundTripDemo
      Expected round-trip output
      Mapped values preserved: true
Same Java object: false
Excluded password after read is null: true
Saved XML: {your project path}/target/roundtrip.xml

      The test compares all five mapped values, using equals for Strings and Double.compare for the GPA. before == after checks identity; it should be false. The same JAXBContext is reused, with a separate writer/reader. Both have the selected schema attached. Changing after in memory will not update the file until you marshal again, just as changing a detached object does not automatically update the database.

      This trace is a browser teaching aid, not a Java execution or grade. Predict the next step, reveal it, then point to your actual code/output.

        CLOSE THE EXAMPLE • PROVE YOUR OWN ROUND-TRIP

        For your RoomCard, compare each mapped value after reading and compare the object references. Test a name containing &. Explain why XML indentation and attribute ordering are weak evidence compared with the parsed values.

        5.8 Read the acceptance schema as a set of rules

        XSD means XML Schema Definition. It describes allowed names, structure, types and constraints. Our chosen student_schema.xsd is more restrictive than the schema JAXB can infer from Student alone.

        session5-jaxb/student_schema.xsd
        <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<xs:schema xmlns:xs="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema">
    <!-- No targetNamespace: this lab's root and children are unqualified. -->
    <xs:simpleType name="NameText"><xs:restriction base="xs:string"><xs:minLength value="1"/><xs:maxLength value="60"/></xs:restriction></xs:simpleType>
    <xs:simpleType name="EmailText"><xs:restriction base="xs:string"><xs:minLength value="1"/><xs:maxLength value="80"/></xs:restriction></xs:simpleType>
    <xs:simpleType name="CourseCode"><xs:restriction base="xs:string"><xs:enumeration value="ADSE"/><xs:enumeration value="DISM"/></xs:restriction></xs:simpleType>
    <xs:simpleType name="GpaValue"><xs:restriction base="xs:double"><xs:minInclusive value="0"/><xs:maxInclusive value="4"/></xs:restriction></xs:simpleType>
    <xs:simpleType name="StudentId"><xs:restriction base="xs:int"><xs:minInclusive value="1"/></xs:restriction></xs:simpleType>
    <xs:element name="student">
        <xs:complexType>
            <xs:sequence>
                <xs:element name="full_name" type="NameText"/>
                <xs:element name="email" type="EmailText"/>
                <xs:element name="course" type="CourseCode"/>
                <xs:element name="gpa" type="GpaValue"/>
            </xs:sequence>
            <xs:attribute name="id" type="StudentId" use="required"/>
        </xs:complexType>
    </xs:element>
</xs:schema>
        XSD declarationRead it in ordinary words
        xs:schema / xmlns:xsThe document uses the XML Schema vocabulary. xs is a prefix for that vocabulary.
        No targetNamespaceStudent documents have unqualified names. A default namespace added to input would change the agreed vocabulary.
        xs:element name="student" / complexTypeThe root contains structured child data and an attribute.
        xs:sequenceThe children must appear in this order. Elements default to exactly one occurrence here.
        NameText / EmailTextString length is 1–60 / 1–80. These restrictions do not check email delivery or reject every whitespace-only value.
        CourseCode enumerationOnly exact ADSE or DISM values are allowed; lowercase adse is different.
        GpaValue / minInclusive / maxInclusiveThe XML double value is restricted to inclusive bounds 0 and 4.
        StudentId / xs:int / minInclusive=1An integer within xs:int’s range, at least 1.
        use="required"The id attribute must be present when validating against this schema.

        An XSD can express many rules, including ranges and enumerations. Application checks still have a role: this schema permits a length-three spaces-only name, while our StudentRules rejects it. XSD 1.0 also does not express every cross-field business rule. Keep the acceptance schema and application rules consistent and test their boundaries.

        YOU PREDICT • CONTRACT FACTS

        Write expected outcomes for id 1/0/missing, GPA 0/4/4.1/letters, name length 60/61, course ADSE/adse and reversed email/course order. Locate the exact schema line responsible for each prediction.

        5.9 Enable schema validation and then check application rules

        session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/ValidatedStudentReader.java
        package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;

public class ValidatedStudentReader {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        try {
            if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply an optional input XML path.");
            Path input = Path.of(args.length == 1 ? args[0] : "examples/valid-student.xml");
            JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
            Unmarshaller reader = context.createUnmarshaller();
            reader.setSchema(SecureXml.schema(Path.of("student_schema.xsd")));
            reader.setEventHandler(event -> false); // Stop on the first validation problem.
            Student student = SecureXml.read(reader, input);
            StudentRules.check(student); // Check application rules before using the object.
            System.out.println("ACCEPTED: " + student);
        } catch (Exception problem) {
            Throwable clue = problem;
            while (clue.getCause() != null) clue = clue.getCause();
            System.err.println("REJECTED: " + clue.getMessage());
            System.exit(2);
        }
    }
}
        session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/StudentRules.java
        package com.aptech.jaxb;
public class StudentRules {
    public static void check(Student student) {
        if (student == null || student.getId() < 1)
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Student id must be positive.");
        text(student.getFullName(), "Full name", 60);
        text(student.getEmail(), "Email", 80);
        if (!"ADSE".equals(student.getCourse()) && !"DISM".equals(student.getCourse()))
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Course must be ADSE or DISM.");
        if (!Double.isFinite(student.getGpa()) || student.getGpa() < 0 || student.getGpa() > 4)
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("GPA must be a finite number from 0 to 4.");
    }
    private static void text(String value, String label, int limit) {
        if (value == null || value.isBlank() || value.length() > limit)
            throw new IllegalArgumentException(label + " must contain 1 to " + limit + " characters and not be blank.");
    }
}
        CodeWhy it matters
        reader.setSchema(...)Attach the chosen acceptance schema; newInstance alone does not do this.
        setEventHandler(event → false)A lambda returning false stops conversion at a validation problem. We reject rather than continue with a partial result.
        SecureXml.readParse using the supplied bounded local-input configuration and map the object.
        StudentRules.checkCheck ordinary Java rules before accepting/using the result, including blank text and finite GPA.
        Double.isFiniteReject NaN or infinity as application data; representability as a double is not sufficient.
        "ADSE".equals / &&Reject when neither permitted course matches. Constant-first equals remains safe for null.
        catch / System.exit(2)Report a rejection and exit unsuccessfully. ACCEPTED prints only after all checks pass.
        $LASTEXITCODE in PowerShellThe last native program’s exit status. In this reader, 0 indicates success and 2 indicates rejection.
        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.ValidatedStudentReader
        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.ValidatedStudentReader examples/missing-course.xml
        $LASTEXITCODE

        Valid input prints ACCEPTED and its mapped values. Missing course prints REJECTED with an XSD clue naming the expected course, and exit code 2. The exact validator wording may differ; do not memorise a version-specific message.

        session5-jaxb/examples/invalid-gpa.xml
        <student id="101">
    <full_name>Amina</full_name>
    <email>learner@example.com</email>
    <course>ADSE</course>
    <gpa>4.1</gpa>
</student>
        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.ValidatedStudentReader examples/invalid-gpa.xml
        session5-jaxb/examples/blank-name.xml
        <student id="101">
    <full_name>   </full_name>
    <email>learner@example.com</email>
    <course>ADSE</course>
    <gpa>3.5</gpa>
</student>
        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.ValidatedStudentReader examples/blank-name.xml

        4.1 is rejected by the schema range. The spaces-only name passes this XSD’s length check but is rejected by StudentRules with a Full name reason. Neither case prints ACCEPTED. No file/database insert happens in this reader. If a later application saves the object, save only after acceptance.

        5.10 Generate a schema and generate model classes

        DirectionWhat is generatedWhat you still decide
        Java → XSDSchema based on Java mapping metadata. This is the direction served by schemagen tooling or JAXBContext.generateSchema.Domain restrictions and the partner’s acceptance contract.
        XSD → JavaXJC writes mapped classes according to the input schema.How application code uses the classes and validates/handles failures.

        A. Inspect a schema inferred from Student

        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.SchemaExportDemo
        Get-ChildItem target/generated-schema
        Get-Content target/generated-schema/schema1.xsd

        This example writes schema1.xsd. Find student, full_name, sequence, xs:double and required id. Then compare it with student_schema.xsd: the generated schema has no course enumeration, name-length or GPA-range facets. Plain Student fields/required annotations did not encode those restrictions. Generating a schema is not proof that it matches your domain contract.

        Read the schema-export API setup
        session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/SchemaExportDemo.java
        package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.OutputStream;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.SchemaOutputResolver;
import javax.xml.transform.Result;
import javax.xml.transform.stream.StreamResult;

public class SchemaExportDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        if (args.length > 1) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Supply an optional schema output folder.");
        Path folder = Path.of(args.length == 1 ? args[0] : "target/generated-schema");
        Files.createDirectories(folder);
        JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
        List<OutputStream> streams = new ArrayList<>();
        try {
        context.generateSchema(new SchemaOutputResolver() {
            @Override
            public Result createOutput(String namespace, String suggestedName) throws IOException {
                Path file = folder.resolve(suggestedName).toAbsolutePath();
                OutputStream stream = Files.newOutputStream(file);
                streams.add(stream);
                StreamResult output = new StreamResult(stream);
                output.setSystemId(file.toUri().toString());
                System.out.println("Generated schema: " + file);
                return output;
            }
        });
        } finally {
            for (OutputStream stream : streams) stream.close();
        }
    }
}

        SchemaOutputResolver is a callback: JAXB asks where to write each schema. The anonymous class overrides createOutput and supplies an output stream and system identifier. Streams are closed in finally. The explicit stream also works with project paths containing spaces. Reuse this setup; explain the resulting contract facts rather than memorising the callback.

        B. Generate classes from the chosen acceptance schema

        The supplied tools folder has a separate Maven build packaging the XJC 4.0.4 generator and its runtime. JDK 17+ does not provide an xjc command by default. Run these commands from session5-jaxb:

        mvn -f tools/pom.xml package
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force schema-client/target/generated-sources
        java -cp tools/target/session5-xml-tools-1.0.jar com.sun.tools.xjc.XJCFacade -d schema-client/target/generated-sources -p com.aptech.jaxb.generated student_schema.xsd
        Get-ChildItem schema-client/target/generated-sources/com/aptech/jaxb/generated

        Expect generated Student.java, CourseCode.java and ObjectFactory.java. XJC may choose different names for another schema. In this schema, CourseCode is a Java enum representing ADSE/DISM. An enum is a Java type with a fixed set of named values; here the generated constants represent the two permitted course codes. Its value method returns the corresponding XML text. This is why the generated course getter returns a CourseCode rather than an arbitrary String. The generated Student uses FIELD access and a CourseCode property; our handwritten Student uses getter mapping and a String course. Different Java shapes can implement the same wire agreement.

        XJC tool project configuration
        session5-jaxb/tools/pom.xml
        <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<project xmlns="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0" xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xsi:schemaLocation="http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0 https://maven.apache.org/xsd/maven-4.0.0.xsd">
<modelVersion>4.0.0</modelVersion><groupId>com.aptech</groupId><artifactId>session5-xml-tools</artifactId><version>1.0</version>
<properties><maven.compiler.release>17</maven.compiler.release><project.build.sourceEncoding>UTF-8</project.build.sourceEncoding></properties>
<dependencies><dependency><groupId>com.sun.xml.bind</groupId><artifactId>jaxb-xjc</artifactId><version>4.0.4</version></dependency><dependency><groupId>com.sun.xml.bind</groupId><artifactId>jaxb-impl</artifactId><version>4.0.4</version></dependency></dependencies><build><plugins><plugin><groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId><artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId><version>3.13.0</version></plugin>
<plugin><groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId><artifactId>maven-shade-plugin</artifactId><version>3.6.0</version><executions><execution><phase>package</phase><goals><goal>shade</goal></goals><configuration><createDependencyReducedPom>false</createDependencyReducedPom>
<filters><filter><artifact>*:*</artifact><excludes><exclude>META-INF/*.SF</exclude><exclude>META-INF/*.RSA</exclude><exclude>META-INF/*.DSA</exclude><exclude>module-info.class</exclude></excludes></filter></filters>
<transformers><transformer implementation="org.apache.maven.plugins.shade.resource.ServicesResourceTransformer"/></transformers>
</configuration></execution></executions></plugin></plugins></build></project>

        C. Compile a caller against those generated types

        session5-jaxb/schema-client/GeneratedStudentDemo.java
        import java.io.StringReader;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import com.aptech.jaxb.generated.Student;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;
import javax.xml.XMLConstants;
import javax.xml.validation.SchemaFactory;

public class GeneratedStudentDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        // A fixed classroom literal, not arbitrary external input.
        String xml = """
            <student id="205">
                <full_name>Chidi &amp; Ada</full_name>
                <email>chidi@example.com</email>
                <course>DISM</course>
                <gpa>3.0</gpa>
            </student>
            """;
        SchemaFactory schemas = SchemaFactory.newInstance(XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI);
        schemas.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
        schemas.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
        schemas.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");
        JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(Student.class);
        Unmarshaller reader = context.createUnmarshaller();
        reader.setSchema(schemas.newSchema(Path.of("student_schema.xsd").toFile()));
        reader.setEventHandler(event -> false);
        Student student = (Student) reader.unmarshal(new StringReader(xml));
        System.out.println("Generated model: " + student.getClass().getName());
        System.out.println("Full name: " + student.getFullName());
        System.out.println("Course: " + student.getCourse().value());
        System.out.println("GPA: " + student.getGpa());
    }
}

        This caller reads a fixed classroom XML literal and attaches the same XSD. Its import points to the generated Student package. It does not call the handwritten Student or a web endpoint. CourseCode.value returns its XML code. For an arbitrary input-file reader, retain the parser controls from 5.12 rather than replacing the fixed literal with an unchecked file.

        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force schema-client/target/classes
        $s5GeneratedSources = @(Get-ChildItem schema-client/target/generated-sources/com/aptech/jaxb/generated/*.java | ForEach-Object FullName)
        javac -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar -d schema-client/target/classes @s5GeneratedSources schema-client/GeneratedStudentDemo.java
        java -cp "schema-client/target/classes;target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar" GeneratedStudentDemo
        Expected generated-model caller output
        Generated model: com.aptech.jaxb.generated.Student
Full name: Chidi & Ada
Course: DISM
GPA: 3.0

        The PowerShell array holds the generated file paths; @s5GeneratedSources supplies them as separate javac arguments. -d selects the compiled output folder. The quoted Windows classpath joins that folder and the existing runtime JAR with a semicolon; the JAR also contains other demos, but this caller imports generated model types. This is standard schema generation, not AI.

        YOU CHANGE • CONTRACT FIRST

        In a copy of the XSD add CPISM to CourseCode. Regenerate into a new output folder, inspect the enum, compile the caller against that folder and change its fixed XML to CPISM. Validate against that changed schema. Do not edit the generated enum by hand. Explain why a changed input value within an unchanged contract needs no regeneration.

        5.11 Choose the API by the job it performs

        QuestionJAX-RS / Jakarta RESTJAX-WS / Jakarta XML Web ServicesJAXB / Jakarta XML Binding
        What is it?A Java API specification for REST-style HTTP services.A Java API specification for XML web services, commonly SOAP.A Java API specification for XML data binding.
        What is its job?Route resource requests and build HTTP responses.Publish/call operations using a SOAP agreement.Map Java values to XML and back.
        Our runtimeJersey.Metro.JAXB implementation packaged in this lab.
        Messages / descriptionsJSON, XML or other media types; OpenAPI documentation is commonly used.SOAP XML; WSDL plus XSD describes the agreement.XML; XSD can describe/validate mapped data.
        What it does not provide automaticallyA correct database design, authentication policy or safe input handling.Configured security, reliable delivery or ACID database transactions just because @WebService appears.An HTTP endpoint, database persistence, encryption or automatic XSD validation.

        REST is an architectural style; SOAP is a messaging protocol. JAX-RS and JAX-WS are Java APIs used to implement services following those approaches. JAXB is data binding used within applications and often within SOAP runtimes. Choosing JAXB is not choosing a competing remote-service protocol.

        ChoiceUseful strengthCost / limitation to assess
        JAX-RSResource URLs and HTTP methods fit browser/mobile APIs; representations can be compact and cacheable when correctly designed.Contracts, validation, authentication and cache headers require deliberate design. JSON is not proof of REST or a guarantee of speed.
        JAX-WSWSDL and schema support typed tooling and partners that require a SOAP agreement.XML/message standards and client configuration add complexity. WS-* support depends on implementation and configuration; it is not automatic security or an ACID transaction guarantee.
        JAXBMapped types reduce repetitive XML traversal; supports both conversion directions and schema tooling.XML-only binding, mapping/schema maintenance and memory use for large object graphs. For very large documents, deliberate streaming may be more suitable.
        YOU DO • EXPLAIN YOUR CHOICE

        Choose and justify the main tool for (1) a GET /items JSON API, (2) a partner requiring SOAP/WSDL, (3) converting a local XML file into Java data. Then explain how JAXB could be used inside option 2 and why it does not start a server itself. Mention one tradeoff for each choice.

        5.12 Keep mapping, validation and parsing responsibilities clear

        PracticeApply it to this lab
        Consistent access strategyHandwritten Student binds annotated getters with NONE; XJC consistently uses FIELD. Avoid duplicate field/getter mappings.
        Explicit acceptance contractAttach student_schema.xsd; do not assume required=true or generated setters validate every restriction.
        Application acceptanceCheck StudentRules before using data; test spaces, non-finite numbers and boundary values.
        Context and workersReuse JAXBContext when binding the same model within an application. Do not share mutable marshaller/unmarshaller workers between concurrent requests without a deliberate design.
        Input parser controlsThe supplied local-file helper caps size, rejects DOCTYPE, disables external entities/resources and closes the input/reader. XSD validation is not a substitute for parser controls.
        Output choicesUse JAXB escaping, UTF-8 where writing bytes, and compare parsed values. Choose output locations deliberately; save evidence before cleaning target.
        Excluded dataUse fictional values. XmlTransient affects JAXB binding; separately avoid logging excluded values and design any other serializer appropriately.
        Read/reuse the input helper, one responsibility at a time
        session5-jaxb/src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/SecureXml.java
        package com.aptech.jaxb;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;
import javax.xml.XMLConstants;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLInputFactory;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamConstants;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamException;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamReader;
import javax.xml.validation.Schema;
import javax.xml.validation.SchemaFactory;

public class SecureXml {
    public static Schema schema(Path path) throws Exception {
        SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI);
        factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
        factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
        factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");
        return factory.newSchema(path.toFile());
    }
    public static Student read(Unmarshaller reader, Path path) throws Exception {
        if (Files.size(path) > 262144)
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("XML must be at most 262144 bytes in this lab.");
        XMLInputFactory factory = XMLInputFactory.newFactory();
        factory.setProperty(XMLInputFactory.SUPPORT_DTD, false);
        factory.setProperty(XMLInputFactory.IS_SUPPORTING_EXTERNAL_ENTITIES, false);
        factory.setXMLResolver((publicId, systemId, base, namespace) -> {
            throw new XMLStreamException("External XML resources are disabled.");
        });
        try (InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(path)) {
            XMLStreamReader xml = factory.createXMLStreamReader(input);
            try {
                while (xml.hasNext() && xml.getEventType() != XMLStreamConstants.START_ELEMENT) {
                    if (xml.getEventType() == XMLStreamConstants.DTD)
                        throw new XMLStreamException("DOCTYPE is not allowed in this exercise.");
                    xml.next();
                }
                Student result = (Student) reader.unmarshal(xml);
                // Read the remainder: extra roots or malformed trailing XML are rejected too.
                while (xml.hasNext()) xml.next();
                return result;
            } finally { xml.close(); }
        }
    }
}

        Files.size checks this classroom input cap of 262144 bytes (256 KiB). XMLInputFactory creates the JDK StAX reader: a parser that reads XML events. SUPPORT_DTD and external-entity processing are disabled; the resolver rejects external resource requests. The small pre-root loop rejects a DOCTYPE declaration. After mapping, the final loop also checks the rest of the document, so an extra root cannot be ignored. try-with-resources and finally close the file and XML reader.

        SchemaFactory loads the known local schema with external DTD/schema retrieval disabled. Our self-contained schema has no imports. JDK APIs javax.xml.stream, javax.xml.validation and javax.xml.transform keep their javax names; JAXB imports use jakarta.xml.bind. Do not rename the JDK imports to jakarta.

        This helper is reusable parsing setup. In independent work you still need to explain its purpose, keep the controls and adapt its returned mapped type. It does not authenticate a caller or define a production service’s complete resource policy.

        Error clueFirst checkRepair and prove
        jakarta.xml.bind not foundCorrect project pom, successful Maven build and classpath?Use the supplied runtime build; rerun the actual named class.
        Duplicate property mapping / propOrder errorAnnotation access strategy; Java property names in propOrder?Keep one mapping per property; rebuild and inspect XML.
        Unexpected element or namespaceRoot/local name and input xmlns match the current mapping?Restore the agreement and test the repaired input.
        Expected course / range facetSelected XSD and actual input order/value?Fix input to meet the stated requirement; record which rule rejected it.
        Missing course maps to nullUsing the comparison reader without XSD?Use ValidatedStudentReader for acceptance, then application rules.
        Generated class or enum absentSuccessful XJC run, destination folder and javac source array?Generate/compile the current schema; do not patch generated files.
        File not found / unexpected output folderGet-Location and printed absolute output path?Use the project folder or correct explicit input/output path.
        DOCTYPE or size rejectionInput outside this lab’s allowed form/size?Use a small ordinary XML record; preserve the parser controls.
        DEBUG NOTE

        Keep one note: command + folder → prediction → actual error clue → one correction → successful retest → explanation. Use a duplicate input file for deliberate mistakes and keep the valid reference for comparison.

        5.13 Build a loan-ticket converter on your own

        A centre exchanges fictional equipment loan tickets as XML files. Write the model, mapping, chosen schema, validation and conversion yourself. Reuse build/parser setup and your notes; keep completed Student solutions and AI tools closed while writing the application logic.

        PartRequirement
        ModelLoanTicket in package com.aptech.jaxb.loan; root loan_ticket; positive ticket_id attribute.
        Children, in orderitem_name, borrower_label, days, charge_ngn. Use Java property names in propOrder.
        Text rulesItem/borrower label 1–40 characters; reject spaces-only values. Practise ordinary letters/spaces.
        Loan ruledays is an integer 0–14 inclusive; chargeNGN must equal days × 25 illustrative NGN.
        Excluded propertyinternalNote with a fictional value. Keep it out of JAXB XML and terminal descriptions; expect null after a round-trip.
        Schemaloan-ticket.xsd: correct names/order; required id; integer day range 0–14 and charge range 0–350; text length limits.
        Application checkCheck the charge equals days × 25 and the text is not blank. A range-valid charge can still be wrong for its day count.
        ConversionMarshal a valid ticket, validate/read it and compare all five mapped values. Reject invalid data before using it.

        Work through milestones

        1. Draw the Java properties and XML names. Write the input/validation/result rules and test predictions.
        2. Create src/main/java/com/aptech/jaxb/loan/LoanTicket.java and LoanRules.java. Write/check ordinary objects before conversion.
        3. Write loan-ticket.xsd and one valid XML input in your own examples folder.
        4. Create LoanTicketDemo.java. Reuse the context/worker pattern; attach your loan schema. Copy/adapt SecureXml into LoanXml with LoanTicket as its return type/cast, keeping the parser controls and closure. Do not cast a LoanTicket to Student or keep student_schema.xsd as its acceptance schema.
        5. Prove marshal/read equality, different object identity and internalNote exclusion. Test each failure in its own input copy.
        6. Close the solution, explain a new instructor requirement and implement/test it. Repeat a smaller variation the following day.

        Save/build from session5-jaxb. The manifest still starts the Student marshal demo; run your own class explicitly after writing it:

        mvn package
        java -cp target/session5-jaxb-1.0.jar com.aptech.jaxb.loan.LoanTicketDemo
        Required testExpected evidence
        days 0 / 1 / 14Charges 0 / 25 / 350; values preserved after read.
        days -1 / 15Rejected by range validation; no accepted ticket.
        days 2, charge_ngn 25Within numeric ranges, but rejected by the equality business rule; correct charge is 50.
        ticket_id 1 / 0 / missingAccept 1; reject 0/missing.
        Text length 1 / 40 / 41; spaces onlyAccept 1/40; reject 41/blank with the responsible rule identified.
        Ampersand/angle brackets in textEscaped on write; original character values on read.
        Missing child / wrong order / wrong root or namespaceContract failure; identify the relevant name/order requirement.
        internalNote / object identityProperty absent from XML and null after read; before and after are separate objects.
        Marking areaMarksEvidence
        Mapping20Correct access strategy, names, order, constructor and exclusion.
        Conversion / round-trip20Works on actual files and compares all mapped values.
        Schema + application rules25Explicit schema attachment, ranges and charge equality, reject before use.
        Tests / diagnosis15Predicted/actual results, failure clues and one repaired input.
        Independent explanation20Own source, unseen change and next-day recall with help recorded.

        Submit: source, schema and input files; README with exact project/build/run commands; test table with exit codes and useful clues; output XML and round-trip evidence; one debug note; comparison justification and learning log. A checkbox/quiz score is not a substitute for running and explaining the application.

        Small hints after your own attempt

        Put attributes/excluded properties outside propOrder. Use explicit XML names, a no-argument constructor and one access strategy. XSD sequence controls child order. Compare Strings using equals. Reuse the parser helper with the correct return type; check a valid document before testing invalid copies. The charge equality belongs in your Java rules for this XSD 1.0 exercise.

        5.14 Explain it, record evidence and check recall

        Answer, read feedback and explain the reason. Then use your own source, XML, schema and test evidence to demonstrate the skill. These revision questions do not automatically grade independence.

        1. Which direction is marshalling?

        2. Does JAXB by itself publish GET /students?

        3. fullName maps to full_name. Which belongs in propOrder?

        4. What enables XSD validation on this reader?

        5. What does XmlTransient guarantee here?

        6. Why is before == after false after our round-trip?

        7. Does inferred XSD automatically include the 0–4 GPA rule?

        8. A loan has days 2 and charge 25, both within schema ranges. What remains?

        Saved in this browser when available; download a separate copy to submit or keep. Nothing is automatically sent to an instructor. Milestones are self-reported.

        Use the beginner trial observation method to record confusing wording and independent transfer. Actual learner outcomes remain to be observed; working examples alone do not establish that every beginner is ready.

Add three 20–30-minute next-day recall checks. The plan starts around 29–30 hours overall; some beginners need more. The manual's two-hour teaching slots can remain core lessons, with preparation, supported lab periods and assignments scheduled separately.

        AT THE END OF EACH WORK PERIOD
        1. Save source and record the command/folder.
        2. Write one prediction, actual result and explanation.
        3. Mark the exact last completed step and next step.
        4. Stop running services safely before leaving or rebuilding.

        If stuck for about 10 minutes: read the first error, check the folder/file and relevant explanation, try one fix, then ask the instructor for a small hint. Record the hint. Return to an independent variation after the guided retry.

        Trial the lessons with 2–3 real beginners

        Status: prepared, awaiting learner observations. This trial checks whether the explanations work for your students. Technical checks show that examples run; learner observations show whether a beginner understands and transfers them. Do not mark the trial complete until real learners have attempted the tasks and the findings have been reviewed.

        Prepare the learners and workspace

        1. Choose 2–3 learners who have not studied Java. Use labels L1, L2 and L3 in the record.
        2. Give each learner a personal project copy and a separate H2 file. Confirm the printed database path belongs to that copy. Do not share one database or another learner's completed work.
        3. Use the schedule above across multiple meetings. Have each learner read and practise the lesson before its matching trial card. A trial is not a first exposure to unexplained material.
        4. Close completed solutions and AI tools during independent cards. Learners may use their own notes, the compiler, contract documents, supplied build/server configuration and ordinary wsimport generation.
        5. Ask them to say what they predict and what each step means. Observe without writing their application code.

        Use findings to improve the HTML

        1. If two learners hesitate at the same instruction, treat that step as a likely teaching gap. Quote it and add a definition, smaller example, predicted output or folder/startup instruction there.
