            7.16 Look up new words beside concrete examples

            TermMeaning here
            CRUDCreate, Read, Update, Delete.
            Deserialize / serializeRead JSON into Java data / write Java data as JSON.
            Input modelWritable request data, ProductInput; no server id property.
            Immutable response modelProduct fields are fixed after construction; replacement creates another Product at the same key.
            Integer / intNullable object wrapper / primitive whole number.
            ValidationCheck business rules before accepting or mutating data.
            CoercionAutomatic type conversion, such as quoted text becoming a number; this parser disallows it for our inputs.
            ContextResolver / providerSupplies configured framework behaviour; JsonSupport supplies Jackson’s mapper.
            Map key / valueIdentifier / Product; id is not a collection index.
            POSTCreate a new product at the collection route.
            PUT replacementSupply all writable values for an existing target.
            UpsertUpdate if present, create if absent; our PUT does not do this.
            DELETERemove the targeted entry.
            LocationResponse header naming the created item’s URL.
            204 No ContentSuccessful response with no body.
            IdempotentRepeated action has the same intended state effect.
            SynchronizedA shared lock keeps each store operation together when threads overlap.
            Durable persistenceData survives process restart; this Map example does not provide it.
            Before/after evidenceGET the stored state on both sides of a request to prove its actual effect.

            8.2 Draw the formats and agree on the contract

            examples/new-product.xml
            <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<product_input>
    <name>  Cable &amp; Adapter  </name>
    <price_ngn>7500</price_ngn>
    <category>Accessories</category>
</product_input>
            Prepared request body — values, not an HTTP request
            {"name":"Cable & Adapter","priceNgn":7500,"category":"Accessories"}
            PlaceNames and meaningWho handles it?
            Input XMLproduct_input root; name, price_ngn, category children in that order; no namespace or id.XML parser and JAXB mapping with chosen XSD.
            Java inputXmlProductInput has name, Integer priceNgn and category.JAXB fills the object; ProductRules checks and trims it.
            Prepared JSONname, priceNgn, category; numeric price; no id.Jackson writes a Map with explicitly selected keys.
            HTTP collectionPOST /api/products with Content-Type: application/json.Jersey/Jackson validate again and the server assigns id.
            HTTP itemPUT/DELETE /api/products/{actualId}.Use the returned Location/id, then prove stored state with GET.

            Binding connects data names to Java members. XML uses price_ngn; our JSON contract uses priceNgn. Changing a file extension does not convert formats. JAXB handles XML; Jackson handles JSON; Jersey handles the HTTP resource. The &amp; characters in XML decode to a single Java ampersand; JSON can contain that ampersand directly.

            PREDICT

            If you send the original XML to Session 7 with Content-Type: application/xml, predict 415. If you label those same bytes application/json, predict 400 because they are not JSON. In both cases predict unchanged server state. Headers identify a format; they do not translate it.

            8.3 Build a small Gradle conversion program

            Use the new session8-practice folder. It contains four Java classes, one packaged schema, six example XML files and the same pinned Gradle 9.2.1 wrapper as Session 7. Use JDK 17–25; compilation targets Java 17. The program converts local files and exits. It starts no web server.

            Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session8-practice"
            java -version
            javac -version
            .\gradlew.bat --version
            .\gradlew.bat lessonInfo
            .\gradlew.bat build
            .\gradlew.bat installDist
            build.gradle
            plugins {
    id 'java'
    id 'application'
}

group = 'com.aptech'
version = '1.0'

repositories {
    // Optional reuse of the prepared course Maven cache on the instructor's PC.
    if (providers.gradleProperty('courseMavenCache').isPresent()) {
        maven { url = uri(providers.gradleProperty('courseMavenCache').get()) }
    }
    mavenCentral()
}

dependencies {

}

            The java plugin compiles source and copies resources. The application plugin supplies run and the installed launcher. mainClass names the entry class. JAXB implementation 4.0.4 and Jackson 2.15.3 are the same library versions already used in the course. JAXB is not bundled in a modern JDK; the dependencies supply it.

            First setup may download libraries from Maven Central. Offline mode works only with a complete cache. The optional courseMavenCache property points Gradle at an already prepared Maven repository; ordinary learners can omit it. It is a build convenience, not application data. Instructor offline command for this workspace:

            .\gradlew.bat --gradle-user-home "..\.build-cache\gradle-home" "-PcourseMavenCache=../.build-cache/maven" --offline --no-daemon build installDist

            Wait for BUILD SUCCESSFUL. test NO-SOURCE means no automated test classes were supplied; it does not prove conversion or endpoints. Save evidence outside build so clean builds cannot remove your submission.

            8.4 Follow the Java values before reading the whole program

            A reference identifies a Java object. A new object may contain the same values while having a different identity. The bridge reads one object, normalizes its values into another object, writes XML and reads a third object. Compare values with Objects.equals; == asks whether references identify the same object.

            Fragment from ProductBridge — study before the full file
            boolean sameValues = Objects.equals(product.getName(), reread.getName())
        && Objects.equals(product.getPriceNgn(), reread.getPriceNgn())
        && Objects.equals(product.getCategory(), reread.getCategory());

Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
body.put("name", product.getName());
body.put("priceNgn", product.getPriceNgn());
body.put("category", product.getCategory());
            Java partPlain meaning
            boolean and &&Keep a true/false answer; all three value comparisons must be true.
            Objects.equals(a, b)Compare contents while handling a possible null.
            Map<String, Object>Associate text keys with values that may be text or integer objects.
            new LinkedHashMap<>()Create a Map; insertion order makes the three keys easy to read. JSON key order has no business meaning.
            body.put(key, value)Store the named value. Explicit keys choose the API contract instead of blindly serializing every XML property.
            IntegerA nullable whole-number object; missing is different from explicitly supplied zero.
            Path and FilesRepresent filesystem locations and read/write local bytes or text.
            try/catch and throwAttempt work; signal/handle failure. A rejection exits with code 2.
            TRACE ON PAPER

            Write the three Map entries after reading the sample XML. Predict whether priceNgn becomes JSON text or a number. Explain why there is no id entry. Predict sameValues true and product == reread false.

            8.5 Type the XML model and attach its chosen schema

            src/main/java/com/aptech/session8/XmlProductInput.java
            package com.aptech.session8;

import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlAccessorType;
import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlAccessType;
import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlElement;
import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlRootElement;
import jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlType;

@XmlRootElement(name = "product_input")
@XmlAccessorType(XmlAccessType.NONE)
@XmlType(propOrder = {"name", "priceNgn", "category"})
public class XmlProductInput {
    private String name;
    private Integer priceNgn;
    private String category;

    public XmlProductInput() { }

    @XmlElement(name = "name", required = true)
    public String getName() { return name; }
    public void setName(String name) { this.name = name; }

    @XmlElement(name = "price_ngn", required = true)
    public Integer getPriceNgn() { return priceNgn; }
    public void setPriceNgn(Integer priceNgn) { this.priceNgn = priceNgn; }

    @XmlElement(name = "category", required = true)
    public String getCategory() { return category; }
    public void setCategory(String category) { this.category = category; }
}
            DeclarationJob
            @XmlRootElement(name = "product_input")Map the expected XML root to this class.
            @XmlAccessorType(XmlAccessType.NONE)Bind only the members explicitly annotated here.
            @XmlType(propOrder = {...})Choose Java property order for XML output. Use priceNgn here, not price_ngn.
            @XmlElement(name = "price_ngn", required = true)Map the XML child to the priceNgn getter/setter. Required metadata is not automatic input validation by itself.
            Public no-argument constructorLet JAXB create an instance before setting mapped values.
            Getter / setterRead a field / assign a field while mapping. This mutable input is separate from Session 7’s immutable stored Product.
            src/main/resources/product-input.xsd
            <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<xs:schema xmlns:xs="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema">
    <!-- Trimmed text length/nonblank checks belong to ProductRules. -->
    <xs:simpleType name="WholeNaira">
        <xs:restriction base="xs:int">
            <xs:minInclusive value="0"/>
            <xs:maxInclusive value="1000000"/>
        </xs:restriction>
    </xs:simpleType>
    <xs:element name="product_input">
        <xs:complexType>
            <xs:sequence>
                <xs:element name="name" type="xs:string"/>
                <xs:element name="price_ngn" type="WholeNaira"/>
                <xs:element name="category" type="xs:string"/>
            </xs:sequence>
        </xs:complexType>
    </xs:element>
</xs:schema>

            XSD means XML Schema Definition. xs:sequence requires the listed children in that order; the default occurrence count makes each required exactly once. xs:int requires an XML integer, and the inclusive limits allow 0 through 1000000. Extra children/attributes such as id, wrong root/namespace and missing fields are rejected by the attached schema.

            The XSD deliberately leaves name/category as strings. ProductRules checks their trimmed length and blank values. XML lexical rules differ from JSON: an XML integer can use surrounding whitespace or a leading plus. The bridge maps it to Integer and Jackson emits a JSON number. A decimal price such as 7500.5 still fails the XSD.

            TYPE → CHANGE → EXPLAIN

            In a personal copy change the XML child mapping to cost_ngn while leaving the XSD unchanged. Predict failure, rebuild and observe it, then align the model/schema/input contract and retest. Explain why changing only the Java field name is insufficient. Keep the reference files intact.

            8.6 Check application rules and retain the parser helper

            src/main/java/com/aptech/session8/ProductRules.java
            package com.aptech.session8;

public class ProductRules {
    public static void check(XmlProductInput input) {
        if (input == null) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Product input is required."); }
        text(input.getName(), "name", 60);
        text(input.getCategory(), "category", 30);
        Integer price = input.getPriceNgn();
        if (price == null || price < 0 || price > 1000000) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("priceNgn must be an integer from 0 to 1000000.");
        }
    }

    public static XmlProductInput normalized(XmlProductInput input) {
        check(input);
        XmlProductInput result = new XmlProductInput();
        result.setName(input.getName().trim());
        result.setPriceNgn(input.getPriceNgn());
        result.setCategory(input.getCategory().trim());
        return result;
    }

    private static void text(String value, String label, int maximum) {
        if (value == null || value.trim().isEmpty() || value.trim().length() > maximum) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException(label + " must contain 1 to " + maximum + " characters after trim.");
        }
    }
}

            check rejects missing input, blank trimmed text, long trimmed text or an invalid price. normalized calls check first, then makes a new input object using trimmed text. Name allows 1–60 and category 1–30 UTF-16 code units after trim, matching Session 7. Use ordinary letters/spaces for beginner length tests. Zero is a valid price.

            Our blank-name fixture satisfies the XSD’s string type but fails the application’s nonblank rule. This is a concrete reason to keep both checks. The Jersey service validates the resulting JSON again; a client-side converter is not authority to skip server validation.

            src/main/java/com/aptech/session8/XmlSupport.java
            package com.aptech.session8;

import java.io.ByteArrayInputStream;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;
import javax.xml.XMLConstants;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLInputFactory;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamConstants;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamException;
import javax.xml.stream.XMLStreamReader;
import javax.xml.transform.stream.StreamSource;
import javax.xml.validation.Schema;
import javax.xml.validation.SchemaFactory;

// Adapted from the Session 5 parser helper; this reader returns XmlProductInput.
public class XmlSupport {
    private static final int LIMIT = 262144;

    public static Schema schema() throws Exception {
        SchemaFactory factory = SchemaFactory.newInstance(XMLConstants.W3C_XML_SCHEMA_NS_URI);
        factory.setFeature(XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING, true);
        factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
        factory.setProperty(XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");
        try (InputStream input = XmlSupport.class.getResourceAsStream("/product-input.xsd")) {
            if (input == null) { throw new IllegalStateException("product-input.xsd resource is missing."); }
            return factory.newSchema(new StreamSource(input));
        }
    }

    public static XmlProductInput read(Unmarshaller reader, Path path) throws Exception {
        try (InputStream input = Files.newInputStream(path)) {
            return read(reader, input.readNBytes(LIMIT + 1));
        }
    }

    public static XmlProductInput read(Unmarshaller reader, byte[] bytes) throws Exception {
        if (bytes.length > LIMIT) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("XML must be at most 262144 bytes."); }
        XMLInputFactory factory = XMLInputFactory.newFactory();
        factory.setProperty(XMLInputFactory.SUPPORT_DTD, false);
        factory.setProperty(XMLInputFactory.IS_SUPPORTING_EXTERNAL_ENTITIES, false);
        factory.setXMLResolver((publicId, systemId, base, namespace) -> {
            throw new XMLStreamException("External XML resources are disabled.");
        });
        try (InputStream input = new ByteArrayInputStream(bytes)) {
            XMLStreamReader xml = factory.createXMLStreamReader(input);
            try {
                while (xml.hasNext() && xml.getEventType() != XMLStreamConstants.START_ELEMENT) {
                    if (xml.getEventType() == XMLStreamConstants.DTD) {
                        throw new XMLStreamException("DOCTYPE is not allowed in this exercise.");
                    }
                    xml.next();
                }
                XmlProductInput result = (XmlProductInput) reader.unmarshal(xml);
                while (xml.hasNext()) { xml.next(); }
                return result;
            } finally { xml.close(); }
        }
    }
}

            This helper adapts Session 5’s parser support to return XmlProductInput. Read its major boundaries: load the bundled XSD, read at most 262145 bytes to detect a 262144-byte limit, disable external XML resources, reject DOCTYPE, unmarshal and read the remainder to catch malformed trailing XML. The JDK supplies javax.xml stream/schema APIs; the separately supplied JAXB API uses jakarta.xml.bind.

            The resource path /product-input.xsd means inside the classpath, not the computer’s drive root. Gradle copies src/main/resources into the application. The schema therefore travels with the installed launcher, while your input/output paths remain relative to the current terminal folder. Streams and parser objects are closed in try/finally blocks.

            EXPLAIN THE ORDER

            What happens to a blank name? What happens to a missing price? Which check catches a trailing second root? Point to where the schema is attached in ProductBridge below. Schema construction alone does not activate validation for an Unmarshaller.

            8.7 Run the conversion and prove the XML round-trip

            src/main/java/com/aptech/session8/ProductBridge.java
            package com.aptech.session8;

import java.io.StringWriter;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Objects;
import com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper;
import jakarta.xml.bind.JAXBContext;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Marshaller;
import jakarta.xml.bind.Unmarshaller;
import javax.xml.validation.Schema;

public class ProductBridge {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        try {
            if (args.length != 2) {
                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Usage: ProductBridge <input.xml> <new-output-folder>");
            }
            Path source = Path.of(args[0]);
            Path output = Path.of(args[1]);
            if (Files.exists(output)) {
                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Output folder already exists. Choose a new folder; earlier evidence is preserved.");
            }
            JAXBContext context = JAXBContext.newInstance(XmlProductInput.class);
            Schema schema = XmlSupport.schema();
            Unmarshaller reader = context.createUnmarshaller();
            reader.setSchema(schema);
            reader.setEventHandler(event -> false);
            XmlProductInput product = ProductRules.normalized(XmlSupport.read(reader, source));

            Marshaller writer = context.createMarshaller();
            writer.setSchema(schema);
            writer.setProperty(Marshaller.JAXB_FORMATTED_OUTPUT, true);
            StringWriter xml = new StringWriter();
            writer.marshal(product, xml);
            XmlProductInput reread = XmlSupport.read(reader, xml.toString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            ProductRules.check(reread);
            boolean sameValues = Objects.equals(product.getName(), reread.getName())
                    && Objects.equals(product.getPriceNgn(), reread.getPriceNgn())
                    && Objects.equals(product.getCategory(), reread.getCategory());
            if (!sameValues) { throw new IllegalStateException("Round-trip changed the product values."); }

            Map<String, Object> body = new LinkedHashMap<>();
            body.put("name", product.getName());
            body.put("priceNgn", product.getPriceNgn());
            body.put("category", product.getCategory());
            String json = new ObjectMapper().writerWithDefaultPrettyPrinter().writeValueAsString(body);
            String report = "ACCEPTED\nSchema and application rules passed.\nRound-trip values preserved: " + sameValues
                    + "\nSame Java object: " + (product == reread)
                    + "\nPrepared JSON contains no id.\nNo HTTP request was sent.\n";

            // Do not publish an output folder until validation and conversions have succeeded.
            Path parent = output.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
            Files.createDirectories(parent);
            Files.createDirectory(output);
            Files.writeString(output.resolve("normalized.xml"), xml.toString(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
            Files.writeString(output.resolve("product.json"), json, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
            Files.writeString(output.resolve("report.txt"), report, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
            System.out.print(report);
            System.out.println("Output: " + output.toAbsolutePath());
        } catch (Exception failure) {
            Throwable clue = failure;
            while (clue.getCause() != null) { clue = clue.getCause(); }
            System.err.println("REJECTED: " + (clue.getMessage() == null ? clue.getClass().getSimpleName() : clue.getMessage()));
            System.exit(2);
        }
    }
}
            Program phaseWhy it occurs here
            Check two arguments and a new output directory.Prevent accidental reuse/overwrite of earlier evidence.
            Create context; attach schema and fail on validation events.Read input against this contract before using it.
            Read and normalize using ProductRules.Only accepted data reaches later conversion.
            Attach schema to Marshaller; write XML into StringWriter.Prepare schema-valid normalized XML in memory.
            Read that XML again and compare all three values.Prove conversion preserves data, rather than just displaying plausible text.
            Choose the JSON Map keys and serialize with Jackson.Produce the exact Session 7 input shape and normal JSON escaping.
            Create output folder and write three files.Publish prepared evidence only after validation and conversion succeed.
            .\gradlew.bat run --args="examples/new-product.xml evidence/product-a"
            $LASTEXITCODE
            Get-Content evidence/product-a/report.txt
            Get-Content evidence/product-a/normalized.xml
            Get-Content evidence/product-a/product.json
            Expected successful program report (absolute output path varies)
            ACCEPTED
Schema and application rules passed.
Round-trip values preserved: true
Same Java object: false
Prepared JSON contains no id.
No HTTP request was sent.

            Check $LASTEXITCODE immediately after the Java/Gradle command when recording it; the success code is 0. The normalized XML has Cable &amp; Adapter without surrounding spaces; reading it gives Cable & Adapter. JSON retains the number 7500 and the three exact API keys. Indentation/JSON key order are not round-trip criteria. The report compares decoded values.

            Try the installed launcher

            .\build\install\session8-practice\bin\session8-practice.bat examples/updated-product.xml evidence/product-b

            Expect the Plus name, 8500 and Devices in product-b/product.json. The launcher runs Java with all dependency JARs without invoking Gradle. It still needs both arguments. If an output folder already exists, choose a new name such as product-c. Re-running an accepted input prepares another local file; it sends no POST and does not allocate a server id.

            Write a personal XML file containing a quote in the name and an escaped ampersand. Predict decoded Java text and valid JSON escaping before conversion. Inspect both outputs and prove all three values survive a read. Use a new output directory.

            A failure prints a clue on the error stream and exits 2. Gradle run reports its task failed when the child exits 2; the installed launcher exposes the Java exit directly.             8.10 Replace and delete using the actual server id

            The product-b output from the installed launcher contains all writable fields for a replacement. Keep the same running server and use the id created in the preceding POST. If you restart A, create a new item and record its returned id before attempting replacement.

            Get-Content evidence/product-b/product.json
            curl.exe -i -X PUT http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products/4 -H "Content-Type: application/json" --data-binary "@evidence/product-b/product.json"
            curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products/4
            curl.exe -i "http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products?category=Accessories"
            curl.exe -i "http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products?category=Devices"
            curl.exe -i -X DELETE http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products/4
            curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products/4
            curl.exe -i -X DELETE http://127.0.0.1:8099/api/products/4

            PUT gives 200, same id/count, Cable & Adapter Plus, 8500 and Devices. The old Accessories filter should exclude this item; Devices should include it. DELETE gives 204 with an empty body, then GET and repeated DELETE give 404. Other products remain. A repeated PUT does not add another item; repeated POST does create another id.

              8.14 Use the symptom to locate the failed layer

              SymptomNext useful check
              Class or JAXB API not foundBuild with the wrapper; use Gradle run or installDist with its complete lib directory. Modern JDKs do not bundle JAXB.
              Gradle cannot resolve a dependency offlinePrepare a complete cache first; check the optional repository property/path. First normal setup uses Maven Central.
              Input file not foundGet-Location and Test-Path; paths are relative to the terminal folder.
              Output folder already existsChoose a new output folder and retain earlier evidence.
              Unexpected root, order or childCompare actual XML and the packaged XSD/mapping.
              Bare ampersand or extra rootRepair XML syntax in your personal input; escaping is part of XML format.
              Blank name rejected after XML type passesProductRules performs the nonblank/trimmed length check.
              Old schema after editingSave source/resources, rebuild and regenerate installDist before using the installed launcher.
              No new product after ACCEPTEDThe converter sent no request. POST its JSON to the running service and follow Location.
              Connection refused / 405Start Session 7 A and wait; verify port 8099 and full CRUD mode.
              415 / 400 when sending XMLCheck actual bytes and Content-Type; run the converter and send its accepted JSON.
              PUT/DELETE 404 after restartRecreate and use the new actual id; restart clears the Map.
              Blank body after DELETEExpected 204; verify absence with GET.
              Partial output after filesystem failureRead the error; do not send a file from a failed run. Choose a new folder after correcting permissions/space.

              Input/schema/rule failures occur before output publication. A later filesystem failure can leave a partially written directory, so only use files from a run that exited 0 and inspect them. No transaction joins local file writes and later HTTP requests. The guided workflow handles one product per run, not an atomic batch import.

              DEBUG NOTE

              Write symptom → hypothesis → evidence → one fix → retest. Explain what failed before changing unrelated files. Keep the smallest useful clue, then demonstrate your successful and rejected cases.

              8.17 Look up the words used in this practice

              TermMeaning and example
              BindingConnect format names to Java members: price_ngn → priceNgn.
              Schema / validationStructural/type contract / check a value against rules.
              NormalizationPut accepted values into a consistent form, such as trimming ordinary spaces.
              Round-tripWrite data to XML, read it again and compare mapped values.
              Object identityWhether two references identify the same instance; distinct instances can have equal data.
              SerializationWrite Java data as format text; Jackson creates the request JSON.
              Classpath resourceA file packaged with classes, such as product-input.xsd.
              CLI argumentText supplied to a program, such as its input and new output paths.
              Exit code / HTTP statusA local process result / a server response code; they are different signals.
              Conditional application ruleStudio seats ≤ 40 despite the generic seats range 1–120.
              LocationAddress of the product assigned an id by successful POST.
              IdempotencyRepeated action has the same intended state effect, such as complete PUT or deleting an absent item.
              Evidence / recallActual observable result / explaining and doing a changed task again later.

              XML SUPPLEMENT

              Optional XML, Namespaces & Java Parsing (DOM vs SAX)

              Supplementary reading retained from the earlier portal. The official teaching Session 2 is the JAX-RS lesson above. These parser examples live in session2-xml-processing and are separate from the new REST project.

              2.1 Anatomy of an XML File

              XML (eXtensible Markup Language) is the data format that powers enterprise web services. Unlike HTML (which is about how text looks), XML is about what the data means.

              1<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?> ➔ The XML Declaration (tells the parser this is XML)
              2<students xmlns:apt="http://www.aptech.com/schema"> ➔ The Root Element (must enclose all other tags)
              3 <student id="101" course="ADSE"> ➔ Child Element with two Attributes (id and course)
              4 <name>Amina Bello</name> ➔ Child Tag containing text value
              5 <apt:grade>Distinction</apt:grade> ➔ Namespace-prefixed Element (apt:)
              6 </student>
              7</students> ➔ Root Closing Tag

              2.2 Why Do We Need XML Namespaces? (`xmlns`)

              🏷️

              The Last Name Analogy

              If a teacher yells: "John, stand up!" in a class with two students named John, confusion happens! But if the teacher says: "John Smith" vs "John Doe", there is no ambiguity.

              In XML, two different companies might both create a tag called <table>:

              • Furniture Company: <furn:table> (a wooden table with legs)
              • HTML / Document: <html:table> (a grid of rows and columns)

              An XML Namespace (like xmlns:apt="http://www.aptech.com") gives tags a unique "last name" or family prefix so different systems can combine XML data without collisions.

              2.3 DOM Parser: Loading the Entire Tree in RAM

              DOM (Document Object Model) reads the entire XML file and creates a tree structure in memory. It allows you to travel up to parents, down to children, and modify any node.

              session2-xml-processing/src/main/java/com/aptech/session2/DomParserDemo.java 
              package com.aptech.session2;

import java.io.File;
import javax.xml.parsers.DocumentBuilder;
import javax.xml.parsers.DocumentBuilderFactory;
import org.w3c.dom.Document;
import org.w3c.dom.Element;
import org.w3c.dom.Node;
import org.w3c.dom.NodeList;

public class DomParserDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        try {
            File xmlFile = new File("session2-xml-processing/students.xml");
            
            // 1. Create DocumentBuilder
            DocumentBuilderFactory factory = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance();
            DocumentBuilder builder = factory.newDocumentBuilder();
            
            // 2. Parse XML file into memory tree
            Document doc = builder.parse(xmlFile);
            doc.getDocumentElement().normalize();

            System.out.println("Root tag: " + doc.getDocumentElement().getNodeName());

            // 3. Extract all <student> tags
            NodeList list = doc.getElementsByTagName("student");
            for (int i = 0; i < list.getLength(); i++) {
                Node node = list.item(i);
                if (node.getNodeType() == Node.ELEMENT_NODE) {
                    Element element = (Element) node;
                    System.out.println("ID: " + element.getAttribute("id"));
                    System.out.println("Name: " + element.getElementsByTagName("name").item(0).getTextContent());
                }
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
}

              2.4 SAX Parser: Event Streaming with Lower Memory Usage

              SAX (Simple API for XML) does not build a complete document tree. It reads a stream and fires events whenever it sees an opening tag, characters, or a closing tag.

              session2-xml-processing/src/main/java/com/aptech/session2/SaxParserDemo.java 
              package com.aptech.session2;

import java.io.File;
import javax.xml.parsers.SAXParser;
import javax.xml.parsers.SAXParserFactory;
import org.xml.sax.Attributes;
import org.xml.sax.SAXException;
import org.xml.sax.helpers.DefaultHandler;

public class SaxParserDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        try {
            File xmlFile = new File("session2-xml-processing/students.xml");
            SAXParserFactory factory = SAXParserFactory.newInstance();
            SAXParser saxParser = factory.newSAXParser();

            // Handler receives events as the parser reads through the file
            DefaultHandler handler = new DefaultHandler() {
                boolean bName = false;

                public void startElement(String uri, String localName, String qName, Attributes attributes) {
                    if (qName.equalsIgnoreCase("student")) {
                        System.out.println("Started Student ID: " + attributes.getValue("id"));
                    } else if (qName.equalsIgnoreCase("name")) {
                        bName = true;
                    }
                }

                public void characters(char[] ch, int start, int length) {
                    if (bName) {
                        System.out.println("Student Name: " + new String(ch, start, length));
                        bName = false;
                    }
                }

                public void endElement(String uri, String localName, String qName) {
                    if (qName.equalsIgnoreCase("student")) {
                        System.out.println("Finished reading Student.\n");
                    }
                }
            };

            saxParser.parse(xmlFile, handler);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
}

              2.5 When to Choose DOM vs SAX?

              Feature DOM Parser SAX Parser
              Memory Usage High (Stores entire tree in RAM) Lower (Streams data; application buffering still uses memory)
              Speed Slower for huge files due to object overhead. Fast and immediate.
              Navigation Bi-directional (can go up, down, backwards). Forward only (cannot go backwards).
              Modifying XML Yes (can add, remove, and edit nodes). Read-only (cannot modify the file).
              Best Use Case Small XML files where editing or searching is needed. Large log files or multi-gigabyte data exports.

              How to Compile & Run Everything Yourself

              Use these run summaries alongside each full lesson. Session 4 setup and staged provider commands cover the practice project.

              Begin in the session1-intro/beginner folder. The full lesson includes expected output and error help.

              Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session1-intro\beginner"

              java HelloJava

              For the web-service lab, compile and start in terminal A:

              javac --add-modules jdk.httpserver FirstWebService.java
              java --add-modules jdk.httpserver FirstWebService

              Leave it running; in terminal B:

              curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8081/greeting

              Press Ctrl+C in terminal A to stop. Read the full guided HTTP lab or fix an error. The older raw-socket demo is optional enrichment.

              In terminal A, enter the Maven project and build:

              Set-Location "C:\Projects\Aptech\creating service\session2-jaxrs"
              mvn package

              Start full mode (append --hello-only for the first greeting exercise):

              java --add-modules jdk.httpserver -jar target/session2-jaxrs-1.0.jar

              In terminal B:

              curl.exe -i http://127.0.0.1:8093/api/courses

              For file-body POSTs, B must also enter session2-jaxrs:

              curl.exe -i -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8093/api/courses -H "Content-Type: application/json" --data-binary "@new-course.json"
